# The Japan Year Book 1907 — Who's Who & Directories

1,326 entries, LLM-transcribed. These entries were transcribed by the vision model of Opus 5.5. Before using any of these details, you must verify specific details with the original source which is linked to whenever possible.


## Who's Who in Japan

**ABE, Taizo** b. '49 at Toyohashi; entered Keiogijiku '68, was its teacher for some years, then at the Imp. Univ. Tokyo when 22nd years old; visited U.S.A. '77 under Viscount (then Mr.) Tanaka, Privy Councillor, and on returning home the following year he resigned Gov. post and started the Meiji Life Insurance Company in 1881; then founded ten years later the Meiji Fire Insurance Co., pioneer concerns in Japan. He is president of the two. Add. Mita, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n109/mode/1up))

**ADACHI, Tsunayuki** ex-Inspector-General of the Metropolitan Police Board, b. in Satsuma, Nov., '59; filled post of chief Police Commissioner at several provinces; was appointed Director of the Police Bureau, Home office in '01; the post of the Inspector-General he had to resign in Oct. '05 in consequence of the celebrated Sept. 5th anti-peace agitation. Add. Odawara, Kanagawa-ken. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n109/mode/1up))

**AKABANE, Shiro** ex-Japanese Minister to Madrid, was b. at Ai- — p. 64 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n109/mode/1up))

**AKAMATSU, Noriyoshi** Baron (cr. '87), Vice-Admiral (retired), M. of the House of Peers, was b. at Shizuoka, Nov. '41; was sent, together with Admi. Vis. Enomoto by the Tokugawa-Government to Holland to study naval theory and art from 1861 to about '66, so that he is a pioneer naval officer in Japan; promoted Rear-Admiral '74; Commander-in-Chief of Sasebo Naval Station and of Yokosuka Naval Station, &c.; Vice-Admiral '87 when he retired. Add. Mitsuke-machi, Shizuoka. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n109/mode/1up))

**AKIMOTO** Viscount, (cr. '84), formerly Daimyo of Tatebayashi in the province of Kozuke, b. May, '57; Minister Resident and Minister Plenipotentiary from 90 to '92; is a great connoisseur of pictorial art and a member of the Academy of Arts (London). He is one of the few Peers who are interested in party politics, being member of the Constitutional Party. Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 64 ([scan leaf 109](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n109/mode/1up))

**AKIYAMA, Genzo** barrister in Tokyo, b. Kakegawa in '59; graduated Law in the Imp. Univ., Tokyo '79, to be appointed a clerkship at the Justice Dep't; next Judge at various local Courts; Chief of the Nagasaki District Court '87 and next of Yokohama District Court '92; Judge of the Supreme Court '98 which post he soon resigned to open law office in Tokyo and Yokohama. Add. Kogai, Az. Tokyo. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n110/mode/1up))

**AKIYAMA, Teisuke** prop. of the Niroku, and ex-M. P., b. Kurashiki in '68. Graduated Law, Imp. Univ. Tokyo '90, earning his expenses by hard working; appointed junior auditor of the Board of Audit, but he soon resigned and started the present paper in '93. It was suspended in a few years from financial difficulty but was revived '01 on the strength of some sensational story relating to a certain millionaire house. Was elected a M.P. from Tokyo in the extraordinary election of '03 and '04, but was compelled to resign '05 owing to absurd charge of being a Russian spy. Add. Nishikicho, Kanda, T. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n110/mode/1up))

**AMANO, Tameyuki** Dr., Hogakushakushi, economist. Professor of Waseda University, b. in Hizen, Dec. '59; grad. Tokyo University '82; has been devoting himself from that time to teaching at Waseda; sat 1st and 2nd sessions in the House of Rep'tatives, but soon withdrew from the uncongenial atmosphere of practical politics. Wrote several works on economics and history of which "Principles on Economic" is popular; is editor and prop. of the "Oriental Economist," a thrice-a-month journal of high authority and extensive circulation. Add. 3-chome, Iida-machi, T. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n110/mode/1up))

**AMEMIYA, Keijiro** b. in the province of Kai, Sept. '45; once a great power on the Exchange and at present in business circles. He strenuously opposed to the proposal of amalgamating the Tokyo Street R'ly Co. with the Tokyo Electric Railway Co., and carried into practice uniform 3 sen fare system. Resigned the post of chairman of the former Company in Mar.'06 when petition to advance the fare was submitted to the authorities, and the three street electric Cos. in Tokyo were amalgamated, and the 4 sen fare system was adopted. Karuizawa, a well-known summer resort, owes him much for present prosperity. Add. Iida-machi, Tokyo. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n110/mode/1up))

**ANDO, Taro** b. '46 in Tokyo, son of the physician to the Lord of Shima; joined Enomoto's pro-Tokugawa rebellion at Hakodate, and after imprisonment lasting a year he was given office at the Foreign Office and was on the suite of the late Prince Iwakura's memorable envoy early in the era. Consul at Hongkong '74-'85, then at Shanghai '86 and Consul-General '86-'89 at Honolulu where he was converted, returning home as fervent Christian and total abstainer. Subsequently held post of Director of Com. Bureau, Foreign Office, and of Commerce and Ind., Agri. and Com. Dep't. Has lately devoted himself to Japan Temperance and other Christian work. Add. Hommura-cho, Az., Tokyo. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n110/mode/1up))

**AOKI, Viscount** (cr. '84,) Amb. to America b. Jan. 1844, in Choshu, eldest son of Genchu Miura, a village doctor; adopted when about 18 by a physician in ordinary to the Lord of Cho-shu; was sent to Berlin for study by his master '69; appointed a few years after Secretary of Japanese Legation there. He was promoted Minister '75; Vice-Minister of Foreign Affairs '86-'89; Minister for Foreign Affairs '89-91 and '98-1900. Was again accredited to Germany '92-'97 and for short while had charge of a similar duty in England as additional post. Viscountess Aoki is a German lady, and their only child is now Countess Hatzefelt, a celebrated German family. — p. 65 ([scan leaf 110](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n110/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**AOYAMA, Tanemichi** Dr., Igakuhakushi, Prof. of Medicine at the Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b.'59; studied medicine in Germany. He was almost killed by pest at Hongkong in '94 whither he with Dr. Kitazato was despatched by the Government to investigate the disease,and by which he was affected. He is one of the most distinguished experts in internal diseases. Add. Yumi-cho, Hg.,T. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n111/mode/1up))

**ARAI, Ryōichiro** New York Agent of the celebrated silk exporting firm, Kiito Gomei Kaisha (Raw Silk Firm, L'td), is a pioneer Japanese direct exporter of raw silk, having started the business '76 in New York where he has remained with only a short break, devoting himself to the business. Subsequently joined the Doshinkaisha, silk exporting firm established by Japanese, which was soon converted into the present Kiito Gomei Kaisha with expanded scope and with a branch office in Shanghai, Canton, Lyon, New York, etc., till it has grown one of the leading, if not the foremost exporting firm of all Japanese and foreign esablishments in this line. Has been awarded Gold Medal by the Japan Sericultaure and Raw Silk Association. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n111/mode/1up))

**ARAKAWA, Gitaro** Gov. of Nagasaki, b. Tokyo in '62; graduated Col. of Law, Imp. Univ. Tokyo in '84, to enter civil service in the Home Office. Was Councillor at the head office and next Secretary at Toyama, Gumma. Kanagawa; Governor of Tottori '97, Mive '99, Kagawa 1900, and finally the present post. Add. Nagasaki Kencho. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n111/mode/1up))

**ARAKI, Kwampo** Mem. of Society of Arts, London; b. June '31 in Tosa, one of the most distinguished historic painters of the Shijo school; studied the art under the then celebrated Araki Kwansai who ultimately adopted him; was appointed Painter of the fief of Tosa. After the Restoration, he taught painting at Peerage Girls' and other schools, and was recently a Professor of the Gov. Fine Arts Institute '98-'06; was appointed 1900 a member of the Fine Art Committee attached to the Imp. Household. Add. Nishi-machi, St., T. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n111/mode/1up))

**ARICHI, Shinanosuke** Baron Vice-Admiral (retired), Mem. House of Peers, was b. in Choshu, March '43; Lieut.-Commander '72; Commander of Yokosuka Admirality and Kure; then successively Chief of Naval Staff: Commander-in-Chief of Standing Fleet &c., is at present devoting himself to promoting the cause of the Japan Maritime Society of which he is president. Add. Awaji-cho, Kanda, Tokyo — p. 66 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n111/mode/1up))

**ARIGA, Nagao** Dr., Hogakuhashi, b. in Settu, '60; gra[illegible] [illegible]kyo University '82; Secretary to Marquis Ito (then President of Privy Council) '87; afterwards Councillor at the Department of Agriculture and Commerce; took charge of affairs relating to international law during the Japan-China War of '94-'95, and held a similar post in the late campaign. He is a Professor of Waseda University, and has represented the Japan Red Cross Society at several international conferences, the last in London '07. Has written several works on history, diplomacy and international law. Granted 2nd Class Rising Sun and yen 2,000 as War honor. Add. Myogadani, Koi., T. — p. 66 ([scan leaf 111](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n111/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**ARISAKA** Lieut.-General inventor of the Arisaka gun, b. in Choshu, '52. First devoted himself to the study of foreign language, and as teacher he was first connected with a military cadet school. Subsequently transferred to an Arsenal and to an Artillery headquarter and finally appointed Captain (art.) '81; sent abroad about 92; Col. '95; completed a new type of quick-firing mountain gun in Nov. '01 after six years' persistent effort, meanwhile again sent aboard '99 on official business. He was decorated with 2nd Class Rising Sun and grant of yen 5000 for his invention, and was promoted Major-Gen. '01. Was granted 2nd class Golden Kite (war honor.) Add. Tsukuto-Hachiman, U., T. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 112](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n112/mode/1up))

**ARISHIMA, Takashi** ex-Dir. Nippon Railway, b. 42 at Kagoshima; took active part in the stormy days of Restoration; abandoned soldiery and entered civil career '70; appointed Dir. Tax Bureau '72; sent Europe and America '78 to investigate taxation affairs: Customs Chief, Yokohama '82; Dir. Customs Bureau '90; National Debt Bureau '91 which he resigned '93 to take charge of financial affairs of the great house of Shimazu; subsequently entered the 15th (Peers) Bank, the Nippon Railway and several other leading establishments. Add. Shimoniban-cho, Koj., T. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 112](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n112/mode/1up))

**ASABUKI Yeiji** b. '49 in Oita-ken, Director of the Mitsui Company; was in youth a hot-headed anti-foreign fanatic, and came up to Tokyo to make attempt on the late Fukuzawa, a pioneer advocate of foreign learning, to be soon converted by him into one of his eager disciples, as even to marry a daughter of his elder sister. After grad. the Keiogijuku, he entered the Mitsubishi; started direct export business of raw silk in which he failed. Next he entered the Mitsui and pushing spinning business (Kanegafuchi Sp. Mill) to the present state of prosperity. Add. Kobiki-cho, Kyo., T. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 112](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n112/mode/1up))

**ASAI, Chu** Prof. Kyoto Higher Technical School and one of the foremost painters of foreign style, b. '56 in Tokyo; studied oil painting at the defunct Art Dep't, Col. Engineering; Pro. of Fine Art Aca., Tokyo, '98, despatched to France to study painting 99-'01; again visited Europe '02. Add. Kyoto Higher Tec. School. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 112](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n112/mode/1up))

**ASADA, Nobuoki** 2nd class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun (war honor). Lieut.-Gen., Com. of the 12th Army Division. b. at Kawagoye '48; grad. Mil. Academy at Osaka '73 and commissioned ensign; fought in the Civil War of '77 as Capt. (local rank) and de- corated with 5th Order; Maj. '84; Lieut.-Col. '87; Col. '94; Major-Gen. '97; and Chief Staff of Eastern Military Section; Com. of a Brigade of various Divisions; took active part as Brig. Com. at Fenshuiling. and succeeded Gen. Hasegawa after Liaoyang as Com. of the Guards Division. Was decorated with 2nd class Mil. Order G. Add. Moji, Fukuoka. — p. 67 ([scan leaf 112](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n112/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**ASADA, Yasunori** Mem. House of Peers, b. in Kyoto. '48; First Class Councillor to Japanese Legation at Washington U.S.A. '74; Councillor of the Department for Foreign Affairs '79; Director of the Bureau of Commercial Affairs '86; Governor of Nagano and next of Kanagawa, '99; Vice-Minister for Foreign Affairs, '00; then that of Communications, which he resigned '02. Add Shimo-6-bancho, Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n113/mode/1up))

**ASANO, Naganori** Marquis, ex-daimyo of the fief of Hiroshima; b. '42; member of the defunct Senate '80; Minister to Rome '82-'84. Add. Yayoi-cho, Hg., T. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n113/mode/1up))

**ASANO Soichiro** President of the Toyo Steamship Co., and Dir. of many other concerns was b. Mar. '48 in Etchu. He arrived in Tokyo '71 with empty purse; proceeded to Yokohama where supported himself by hawking cheap viands. By his shrewdness he bought from the Yokohama Gas Works Coal-tar and other refuses which were then left unutilized, and laid the foundation of the present fortune by utilizing them. In '75 he bought the Gov. Cement Works at Fukagawa and began to produce "Asano Cement" now widely known; started tank oil business '93 and promoted with others the Toyo S.S. Co. '95. Is now an acknowledged power in business world. Add. Shiba Tamachi, Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n113/mode/1up))

**ATOMI, Takino** b. April '40 at Osaka, one of the distinguished lady painters and educators; founder and proprietor of the Atomi Girls' School. Visisted U.S.A. on the occasion of St. Louis Fair. Add. Yanagi-cho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n113/mode/1up))

**BABA, Michihisa** b. '47 at Iwase, Toyama-ken, of a family of ship-owner, is Pres. Iwase Bank and Dir. Tokyo Marine Ins. Co., Imp. Marine Ins. Co., Innoshima Dockyard, and others. Is a millionaire in Toyama and one of the wealthiest individual shipowners in Japan. Add. Tori 2-chome, N., Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n113/mode/1up))

**CHINDA, Sutemi** Vice-Minister of Foreign Affairs, b. at Hirosaki '56; graduated an American University '81; appointed a clerk of the Foreign Office '85 and had charge of the Telegraph Section in the same office; appointed Consul at San Francisco '90; removed to the Consulate at Ninsen; the Consul-Generalship at Shanghai '95; Minister Resident at Brazil, next at the Hague '99-1900; Minister Plenipotentiary and Envoy Extraordinary at St. Petersburg 1900; recalled in '01 by the then Foreign Min. Baron Komura to act as his immediate subordinate, and had to take charge of the Office while his superior was away to Portsmouth and Peking. In recognition of this special service he was invested with 1st class Sacred Treasure. Add. Foreign Office, Tokyo. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n113/mode/1up))

**DAN, Takuma** Manager Mining Dep't of the Mitsui Firm, b. '58 at Fukuoka, 3rd son of one Suwa in that place and adopted into the present family '80; was sent for study to America early in the era by the Lord of Fukuoka and completed the course. Studied mining at the Boston Tech. Institute, U.S.A., and was for some years teacher of English at Gov. school, technical official at the Gov. Meteorological Observatory and next at the Miike Coal-mine, with the purchase of which by the Mitsui he entered the service of the Firm. Add. Tango-cho, Akasaka, T. — p. 68 ([scan leaf 113](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n113/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**DEX, Kenjiro** Member of the House of Peers and ex-Vice-Minister of Communications, b. '55 in Hyogo-ken; held at several places post of local Chief Police Inspector; Vice-Min. of Communications Jan.-Jun. '98; President of the Kansai R'ly Co., at the same time M.P. for Hyogo-ken; was again Vice-Min. of Communications '00-'06. He is now a prominent figure in business circles. Add. Kanasugi, Sb., Tokyo. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n114/mode/1up))

**DEWA, Shigeto** Chief of Naval Education Board, holder of 2nd class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of the Rising Sun; b. at Aizu; grad. the Naval Academy; saved by his presence of mind the training cruiser Ryuki when she started fire in the South Pacific '84 on board of which he was Lieutenant; Vice-Commander of Takao '85; was in time promoted Capt. and was appointed Dir. of Naval Affairs Bureau; granted 4th class Golden Kite for his service in the Japan-China war; was chief of the Committee ordered to England to bring home the cruiser Tokiwa 1899; Rear-Adm. 1900 and as Commander of the Standing Squadron he stayed off Taku at the time of the Boxer's trouble; afterward transferred to Chief Construction Board at Yokosuka, next to Director of Naval Affairs Bureau; Commanded a Squadron in the late war. Add. Mita Tsuna-machi, Sb., T. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n114/mode/1up))

**DOI, Keizo** Dr., Prof. of Med. Col. Imp. Tokyo Univ. and Chairman of the Japan Skin Disease Society; grad. the Med. Col. '90; assist and student of the Univ. Hall at this alma mater; studied his speciality in Austria, Germany and France '95-'98, to be appointed to the present chair on his returning home; was on suit of the late Prince Komatsu when his highness attended the Coronation Ceremony of King Edward '02. Add. Fujimi-cho 4-chome, T'yo. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n114/mode/1up))

**DOI, Michio** Chairman of the Osaka Chamber of Com. b. in Kanagawa '41; was a Judge till '84 when he entered the service of the Konoike family, Osaka, of which he is now an adviser. Add. Konoike, Osaka. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n114/mode/1up))

**EBARA, Soroku** M.P., educationalist, a leader of the Constitutionalist Party, b. at Tokyo in Jan., '42; was first a military officer, but subsequently resigned the commission and was for somewhile Secretary of the Prefecture of Shizuoka. '70. He is chiefly known in conection with subsequent non-official career: has sat several times in the House where he now represents Tokyo. Is Director of the Azabu Middle School, Mem. of the Higher Education Council and is a devout Christian. Add. Torii-saka, Tokyo. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n114/mode/1up))

**ENOMOTO, Buyo** Viscount (cr. 1884) Vice-Admiral (retired), b. Aug. '36 at Tokyo (Yedo); was a pioneer naval officer sent for study to Holland by the Toku- gawa Government; returned home in '66 on board the corvette Kanyo Maru built at Amsterdam to the order of that Government. In the civil war of the Restoration he at the head of that vessel and others fled to Hakodate where, for several months, he with Otori (now Baron) successfully withstood the attack of the Imperial army; he surrendered at the earnest remonstrance of the leader of the Imperial Army and was released after the imprisonment of a year or two, to be at once appointed to an important Government post in St. Petersburg '74 to conclude the treaty of exchanging Saghalien with the Kuriles; was Minister at Peking '82, and sat in almost every Ministrial chair with exception of that of Army, Finance and Justice. Add. Mukojima, Tokyo. — p. 69 ([scan leaf 114](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n114/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**FUJII, Shigeta** Maj.-Gen. and Dir., Art. and Eng. School; 2nd class of Golden Kite and of Rising Sun (war honor), b. '60 in Hyogo-ken; entered the Military Academy '77; Ensign of Artillery '79; entered the Military Staff Col. '84; studied tactics in Germany '90; Staff officer to the 2nd Army in the Japan-China War; Major-Gen. '02 and appointed Dir. Military Staff College, from which he was transferred to post of Chief Staff to Gen. Kuroki's Army in the late war, and restored to the original post on close of the War. Add. Oban-cho, Y., T. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 115](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n115/mode/1up))

**FUJIOKA, Ichisuke** Dr., Kogakuhakushi, b. in Choshu, '56; grad. Tokyo Engineering College, '81; appointed its Professor from '84 to '86 when he retired to start electric business; chief electrician to the Tokyo Electric Light Co. '93 and founder of the Tokyo Street Railway Co. which with two other street railway cos. was fused '06; he being at present its chief engineer. He is a pioneer electric engineer in Japan. Add. Zaimoku-cho, Az., T. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 115](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n115/mode/1up))

**FUJINAMI, Viscount** (cr. '84), b. at Kyoto, Sept. '53; Chamberlain to H.I. Majesty from childhood, and since '89, Superintendent of the Imperial Mews. He has frequently been sent abroad on official business. Was principal promoter of the Horse-race course at Ikegami, near Tokyo. Is Deputy Pre. of Horse Adm. Bureau. Add. Shimo-Nibancho, Koi., T. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 115](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n115/mode/1up))

**FUJISAWA, Rikitaro** Dr. Rigakuhakushi, Prof. of Science of Tokyo University, b. in Echigo, Sept. '61; pursued his special study, that of mathematics, in England and Germany '83-'87. He has written several works on Mathematics. Add. Suwa-cho, Koj., T. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 115](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n115/mode/1up))

**FUJITA, Denzabro** one of the new-made millionaires, b. in Choshu, May '41; promoted the lately nationalized Sanyo Railway Co., and started in Osaka the Fujita Firm to undertake contract business with the Government, beside undertaking mining and engineering works. The reclamation work started at the foreshore of Kojima, Okayama-ken, is now nearing completion, and hundreds of acres will then be added to his estate. Add. Ami-cho, Kitaku, Osaka. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 115](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n115/mode/1up))

**FUJITA, Ryutaro** Chief Judge of the Nagoya Appeal Court, b. '56 at Uwajima, Iyo; grad. Law. Imp. Tokyo Univ. '78; Judge in '81; for sometime a jun. Sec. and Vice-Dir. Commercial Bureau, Foreign Office; returned to the Bench and continues to occupy the present post from '98. Add. Nagoya. — p. 70 ([scan leaf 115](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n115/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**FUJITA, Shiro** Member of the House of Peers, b. at Tokyo, June '61; attaché of Japanese Legation in Vienna '86, then in Berlin; afterwards Private Secretary to the Minister of Communication and of Agriculture and Commerce, &c.; Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Commerce '98; now President of the Nippon Fire Insurance Co. and of other concerns. Add. Mita, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 116](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n116/mode/1up))

**FUKUHA, Bisei** Viscount (cr. '84), former Daimyo of small fief in Iwami, b. in July '31; member of the House of Peers, and a high authority on native poetry. Add. Yodobashi, Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 116](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n116/mode/1up))

**FUKUHARA, Arinobu** Pres. of the Teikoku Life Insurance Co., b. '48 in Awa (now Chiba-ken); started in '72 the Shiseido drug store, one of the leading establishments in Tokyo in this line; promoted with others the above company and has since been chairman of Board of Directors. Travelled through Europe and U.S.A. several years ago to inspect the insurance affairs there. Add. Takekawa-cho, Ginza, T. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 116](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n116/mode/1up))

**FUKUHARA, Ryojiro** Dir. Special School Bureau, b. '69 in Ise, graduated Law Imp. Univ., Tokyo, '92. Held various junior posts at central Government and then transferred to local Offices as Councillor or Chief Police Commissioner; removed to the Education Dep't '98 as Councillor and Inspector; dispatched to Europe '99 to investigate affairs of educational administration and returning home '02 was made Councillor and Section Chief of the Department and promoted to present post in '04. Add. Minami-cho, U., Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 116](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n116/mode/1up))

**FUKUOKA, Viscount (cr. '84), Privy Councillor** b. in Tosa, Feb. '35; after the Restoration of '67 entered civil service and became Minister of Education '81-'82. Appointed '88 to the present post. Add. Kanetomi-cho, Koj., T. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 116](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n116/mode/1up))

**FUKUSHIMA, Yasumasa** holder of 2nd class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun (war honor), Lieut.-General, Vice-Chief of the General Staff Office, b. in Shinano, Sept. '53; entered military service; Lieutenant '78; Captain '83; Military Attaché of Japanese Legation at Berlin '87-'92; Major '88; was welcomed by the populace almost as a triumphant hero when in June '93 he returned home after successfully travelling through Siberia on horseback. Promoted Major-General in '00, and acquired international fame as Commander of the Jpannese contingent on the occasion of the Boxer's trouble, and was knighted by King Edward. Was on the Staff of Marshal Oyama in the late War in which he lost his son. Add. Yarai, U., T. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 116](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n116/mode/1up))

**FUKUZAWA, Sutejiro** born at Tokyo '63, second son of the late celebrated Fukuzawa; was together with his elder brother Mr. Ichitaro Fukuzawa, entered Yale Univ., '83, the younger Fukuzawa to pursue engineering and the elder literature. They both returned home in '90, and Mr. Sutejiro has since been managing the Jiji and the Osaka Jiji started '05. Add. Mita, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 71 ([scan leaf 116](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n116/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**FUKUZAWA, Momosuke** brother-in-law of the above, being the husband of their sister, and b. '64 at Kawagoye, second son of a rich merchant there; studied at the Keio-gijuku where his uncommon ability attracted the attention of its founder; proceeded to U.S.A. after grad. from that institution and studied at a Commercial Col. there. Returning home '89, he first entered the Toko Ice Works, Oji Paper Mill and others, and finally the Hokkaido Colliery and Steamship Co., of which he was Agent for Tokyo, besides sitting on the boards of directors of several other concerns Add. Mita, Tokyo. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 117](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n117/mode/1up))

**FUNAKOSHI, Mamoru** Baron (cr. 1900), M. of the House of Peers, b. at Hiroshima, '40; in '68 he entered civil service; appointed Secretary of the Home Office, then filled successively the Governorship of Chiba, Ishikawa, and of Miyagi, &c.; nominated the Member of the House of Peers '94. Add. Ichigaya Kaga-cho, T. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 117](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n117/mode/1up))

**FURUICHI, Koi** Dr., Kogakuhakushi, Dir .of R'ly Bureau, Res. Gen., Seoul, Member House of Peers, was born at Himeji '54; pursued his engineering study in France '75-'80; filled the posts of the Directorship of the Public Works Bureau, Home office; Vice-Minister of Communications, and Director-General of the Government Railways, which he resigned in '03 in order to take up the present post. He is one of the best amateur nō singers and actors. Add. Yumi-cho, Hg., T. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 117](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n117/mode/1up))

**GA, Reishi** Member of the House of Peers since '91, b. at Nagasaki in July '40; a pioneer scholar of Dutch; held several important posts in the Government service. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 117](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n117/mode/1up))

**GO, Junzo** Baron (cr. 1900), Member of the House of Peers since '90, b. in Mino, '25; entered the civil service in the Finance Department in '68; Director of the Bureau of National Debt, '76; afterwards Secretary of Finance Department; Vice-Minister of Finance, '86, from which he retired in '88. Add. Shimo-Nibancho, Koj., T. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 117](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n117/mode/1up))

**GOTO, Shimpei** Baron (cr. '06), Pres. South Manchuria Railway. b. '56 in Iwate-ken, son of a doctor, and is a nephew of the celebrated patriot Takano who was persecuted to death by the Tokugawa Government a little before the Restration. He studied medicine both in Japan and Germany; was chief of the Nagoya Hospital. Subsequently appointed junior Director of the Sanitary Bureau, Home Office; was arrested on charge of implicity in the notorious Soma scandal to be acquitted about a year afterward as it was proved that he himself was a victim of the author of that scandal, Nishigori, a petty Cagliostro. He got appointment as Sanitary Commissioner at the time of the Japan-China War. after which he re-entered the Sanitary Bureau as its director. Was singled out '97 by the late Vis. Kodama, newly appointed Governor-General of Formosa, as Director of the Civil Administration Bureau which he had continued to occupy till his transfer to the present post '06. His career is identified with the growth and progress of that new dominion. For the rapid development which the new domi- nion has made great credit was due to his energy and foresight, and it was in recognition of this service that he was made a Peer in April '06. From his extraction and career he is a Dr. Jameson of Japan, minus warlike adventure. Add. Tairen (Dalny), Kwant'g. and politician. b. '62 in Kishu: grad. Keiogijuku: attended to the family business of Japanese soy and salt wholeseller; did much to save now flourishing Fuji Cotton Mill from impending ruin, of which he is now a Dir., and has also played active part in business. Was returned three times to the House of Representatives, but has since kept aloof from active politics. Travelled through Europe and America about 1900. Add. Ko-ami-cho, N., T. — p. 72 ([scan leaf 117](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n117/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**GWASSAN** Yagoro, master sword-smith appointed '06 Art Commissioner to Imp. Household, b. '46 in Omi and learned craft under Teikichi Gwassan at Osaka by whom he was ultimately adopted as son and heir; is the greatest master in producing swords of Soshu and Ichimonji style, besides creating a new mode of tempering. He has made swords for the Emperor and the Crown Prince, and his swords have acquired wide renown for perfect elasticity combined with unique sharpness. Add. Yariya-cho, Osaka. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 118](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n118/mode/1up))

**HACHISUKA** Shigeakira, Marq., (cr. 1884; formerly Daimyo of Tokushima), a Privy Councillor, b. '46; represented Japan in France about '81; was appointed President of the House of Peers '91-'96; Minister of Education Sept. '96-Nov. '97; was appointed to the present post the following year. Add. Mita-Tsuna-machi, Sb., T. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 118](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n118/mode/1up))

**HAMADA** Gentatsu. Dr., Igakuhakushi, b. '54 in Kumamoto-ken; studied medicine in Germany '85-'88; Professor of Medical Course of the Tokyo Univ. '89-'1900; is an acknowledged authority on women's diseases, and is the chief and proprietor of the Hamada Hospital for women's diseases. Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 118](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n118/mode/1up))

**HAMAGUCHI** Kichiemon, a millionaire merchant, land-owner [illegible] — p. 73 ([scan leaf 118](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n118/mode/1up))

**HAMAO** Arata. Pre. Imp. Univ. Tokyo and Member of the House of Peers. L.L.D. (Harvard) b. in Tajima, '49: Councillor of the Department of Education '78; Vice-President of Tokyo University in '84: Director of the Special Education Bureau '88; President of Tokyo Imperial University '93-'97: Minister of Education from Nov. to Dec. of 1897. Re-appointed to the present post Dec. '05 with Shinnin rank in consequence of the celebrated "Strike movement" of the Professors. Add. Kanetomi-cho, Koi., T. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 118](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n118/mode/1up))

**HANABUSA, Baron** (cr. '96), Vice-Minister of the Imperial Household Department, Vice-President of the Japan Red Cross Society, etc., b. Okayama '42; Secretary for Foreign Affairs '70; Charged Affairs at Seoul, '76-'77; promoted to Minister Resident, '80-'81, and narrowly escaped being killed by the anti-Japanese Korean mob that rose there. Minister to St. Petersburg, '83-'86; Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Commerce '87-'88, and then transferred to the Imperial Household as its chief auditor, and next promoted to the present post. Add. Tsukiji, Tokyo. rea '96. He resigned the post the following year and for somewhile took to journalism as chief editor of the Osaka Mainichi. He was one of the right-hand men of Marquis Ito when he raised the banner of the Seiyukai '00, and filled as the late Hoshi's successor the chair of the Minister of Communications from Dec. '00 to May the following year. Add. 4, Shiba Park, Tokyo. — p. 73 ([scan leaf 118](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n118/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**HANABUSA, Naosaburo** Dir. Statistic Bureau of the Cabinet, b. '57 in Okayama, brother of the above. studied German under German Prof. Holtz in Tokyo but owing to delicate health could not proceed to Germany though ordered by the Government to do so. On recovery of health he was appointed official of the Cabinet, then Secretary of the Privy Council, Personal Secretary to Marquis Ito. Secretary of the Cabinet, and finally appointed to the present post in '98. He travelled abroad to investigate statistic affairs '02-'03, and is one of the authorities in this line. Was decorated this year with the Middle Cordon of the Rising Sun for his service in the Imperial Family Affairs Commission. Add. Aoyama Minami-cho, T. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 119](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n119/mode/1up))

**HARA, Kei** M.P., Home Minister, b. '54, at Morioka; entered the defunct Law College, Justice Dep't, but left before graduation. He then took to journalism and was on the staff of the Hochi, which he soon left to take charge of a newly started organ of the Clan interest; followed Count Inouye as newspaper correspondent when he was despatched to Korea '84 as Special Envoy, and was appointed the Count's Personal Secretary when he filled the chair of Foreign Affairs. He is a public man who enjoyed a great confidence from the late Count Mutsu under whom he served as Personal Secretary '90-'92 when the statesman was Minister of Ag'ture and Commerce; followed his chief to the Foreign Office as Director of the Com'cial Bureau to be promoted to Vice-Minister of the Dep't '95 and transferred to Minister to Ko- — p. 74 ([scan leaf 119](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n119/mode/1up))

**HARA Rokuro** b. '44 in Tajima and played some distinguished part at the time of the Restoration having fought on the Imperial side in the civil war; sent to Europe '71 by the Government, but soon resigned the appointment. He proceeded to America and made some profit by purchasing the depreciated greenbacks, this enabling him to pursue his study for three years in England, returning home '77. Entered and founded with a few others the One Hundredth Bank and has been instrumental in pushing it to the present flouring condition; filled the post of President of the Yokohama Specie Bank from '83 to '90; is acknowledged as a power in business circles, and is a director for a number of Companies. Add. Takanawa, Tokyo. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 119](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n119/mode/1up))

**HARA, Ryuta** Kogaku-hakushi. b. at Fukuoka, '54; graduated the Engineering Course of the Tokyo University in '81; has taken charge of the building several bridges in Tokyo and also supervised its Water-works; is a Professor of his alma mater as additional duty, his principal duty being Engineer to the City of Tokyo. Add. Sashigaya, Koi., T. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 119](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n119/mode/1up))

**HARA, Tomitaro** Pres. 2nd Bank. Yokohama, b. in Shinshu '70: studied at Waseda Univ., was adopted into the present millionaire family as husband of its only daughter, is a Director of the Yokohama Fire Insurance Co. and is a leading young business-man reputed for energetic mind. Add. Benten-dori, Yokohama. — p. 74 ([scan leaf 119](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n119/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**HABA Yoshimichi** lawyer b. in Shinano, Feb. '97; graduated the Tokyo University in '89; appointed Councillor of the Department of Agriculture and Commerce in '90; afterwards appointed Chief of the Tokyo Mine Inspection Office and that of Osaka. Left Government service in '93 and has since been practising law. He is reputed as being one of the most level-headed barristers and also the most flourishing, of the Tokyo Bar. Add. Iida-machi, Koj., T. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 120](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n120/mode/1up))

**HARADA, Tasuku** Dir. of the once famous Doshisha in Kyoto, b. '63 in Kumamoto; studied at the Foreign Language School in Kumamoto, the Tokyo English School and lastly at the Doshisha which he graduated 84. Appointed Pastor in Kobe; graduated Divinity at Yale, class 91 and the following year travelled through Europe in company of Rev. Griffis, author of the Mikado's Empire, etc.; was Pastor in Tokyo, Kyoto and Kobe, and finally installed in the present chair in '06. Add. Doshisha, Kyoto. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 120](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n120/mode/1up))

**HARUKI, Yoshiaki** Chief Judge of the Tokyo Appeal Court, b. '46 in Yamato; took a distinguished part for the Imperial cause in the Restoration work; junior Public Procurator '73, Judge at the Tokyo District Court '80, presiding Public Procurator at the Nagasaki Appeal Court, which post he soon resigned. Appointed Public Procurator of the Supreme Court '85, Chief Public Procurator at the Hakodate Appeal Court '86 and a similar post at Hiroshima '87, Public Procurator-General at the Supreme Court '92, Chief of the Tokyo Appeal Court '98. Add. Funa-cho, Yotsuya, Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 120](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n120/mode/1up))

**HARAGUCHI, Kaname** Dr., Kogakuhakushi, R'ly. Eng. Adviser to the Viceroy of Hukwang Provinces, China; b. '51 in Nagasaki-ken; was one of the pioneer students sent abroad for study by the Government, having studied Engineering at Yale '75-'79; served long on the Gov. R'ly. service; Dir. of the Hakata Bay R'ly. Co. '02; proceeded to the present post early last year. Add. Nagata-cho, Tokyo. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 120](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n120/mode/1up))

**HARAGUCHI, Kensai** 3rd class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun (war honour), Lieut.-Gen. (ret.), b. 47 in Bungo; Sub.-Lieut. '72; Maj.-Gen. '97 meanwhile having filled the post of Com. of 1st Reg. (Inf.), Body Guards, Chief Staff to the 4th Div. and Dir. of the Toyama Mil. School. Commanded the Saghalien Exp. Army in the late War. He was dispatched to Germany '90 to investigate military affairs. Add. Sanai-cho, U., T. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 120](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n120/mode/1up))

**HASEBA, Sumitaka** M.P., a leader of the Constitutionalist Party, b. Satsuma. '54; joined the Saigo's army in the Civil War of '77 and was imprisoned after its suppression. Was for a long while an administrative official in a district of Kagoshima and is one of the very few M.P.s who sit in the House from its advent in '90. He was appointed chief councillor of the Home Office in the 4th Itō Cabinet. Travelled abroad in '06. He is one of the best shots in Japan. Add. Mamiana, Az., T. — p. 75 ([scan leaf 120](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n120/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**HASEGAWA, Tai** Dr. and publicist, b. in Niigata; was appointed Vice-Dir. of the Medical Col. of Tokyo Univ., '73, and has frequently filled various posts in civil service, the last being that of Dir. of Sanitary Bureau, Home Office. He twice sat in Parliament and for a long while kept a private Medical School which was lately closed. Has lately kept aloof from active life. Add. Yushima, Hg., T. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 121](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n121/mode/1up))

**HASEGAWA** 1st class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun and Poulonia (war honor), General, Baron, (cr. '95), Commander-in-Chief of the Korean Garrison, b. '50 Suwo; Major '72; Colonel '78; Major-General '86; Lieut.-General '96; General in June 1904. He commanded a Brigade in the Japan-China War and took a distinguished part in the battles of Port Arthur and Wei-hai-wei. In the late war he commanded the Imp. Guards Division under Gen. Kuroki and led it to success and honor at the Yalu and the battle of Liaoyang, and then transferred to the present important post. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 121](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n121/mode/1up))

**HASEGAWA, Yoshinosuke** Kogakuhakushi, b. Hizen, '55; studied mining in U.S.A. and Germany from '76 to '80; engaged by the Mitsubishi Firm to take charge of its mining dep't. It was through his ingenious plan that the work of Yoshioka Copper Mine with which the firm was at a loss what to do has been made highly profitable. He has subsequently left the firm to start business on his own account, he owning two or three coal-mines in Kyushu. He sat for a short while in the House of Representatives '02. Add. Kobinata-dai, Koi., T. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 121](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n121/mode/1up))

**HASHIMOTO, Gaho** one of the most celebrated contemporary painters of the Japanese style, b. Tokyo, '35; was Professor of the Gov. Fine Art Institute at Uyeno, from which he with its President Mr. Okakura and several other members of the faculty served connection in '99 and set up the Nippon Fine Art Institute at Yanaka. Gaho's merit lies in landscapes, (vide Chap. Arts and Crafts of Modern Japan.) Add. Tatsuoka-cho, Hg. T. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 121](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n121/mode/1up))

**HASHIMOTO, Tsunatsune** Dr., Baron, b. in Fukui, '45; brother of the celebrated patriot, Sanai Hashimoto, who distinguished himself in upholding the cause of the Imperial House before the Restoration and put to death on that account by the Tokugawa Regency. The Doctor studied medicine in Germany 1872-'77 and was appointed an Army Surgeon soon after his return; promoted to Surgeon-General of the Medical Dep't about '85, resigned two years later to devote himself to the Red Cross work, undertaking the responsible post of the Chief of its Hospital; was nominated member of the House of Peers in '90, but resigned it next year. He is a physician-in-waiting to the Court and is regarded as one of the highest practitioners in internal troubles. Was invested with Grand Cordon of of Rising Sun for his service in the late war. Add. Hirakawa-cho, Koj., T. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 121](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n121/mode/1up))

**HATA, Seikichiro** Chief of Locomotive section of the Government Railways, b. '57 in Sasa- yama; graduated Mech. Engineering '85 at the now defunct Eng. Col., and has since been connected with the present service till rising to the present position. He travelled abroad on official business in '97. Add. Shiodome, Sb., Tokyo. — p. 76 ([scan leaf 121](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n121/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**HATANO, Yoshinao** ex-Minister of Justice, b. Hizen, '50; entered the Department of Justice, '71, Councillor '87; Secretary '88; afterwards President of the Kyoto Local Court and Judge of the Supreme Court &c.; Vice-Minister of Justice in '99; resigned the Ministership Jan. '06. Add. Ukyomachi, Y., T. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 122](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n122/mode/1up))

**HATOYAMA, Kazuo, Dr., L.L.D. (Yale), Hogaku-hakushi, M.P** a leader of Progressive Party, and lawyer, b. Tokyo, '56; grad. Yale University, class '79; Professor of Law of Tokyo University '79-88; Director of the Bureau of Investigation (abolished afterwards) of the Department of Foreign Affairs '85-'88. Has sat in the House of Representatives for Tokyo since '92, and was appointed its President twice. He was appointed Vice-Minister for Foreign Affairs in the Okuma-Itagaki Ministry '98; was granted LL.D. from Yale University '01. He has resigned '07 the chair of President of Waseda University. He is one of the prominent lawyers in Japan. Add. Otowa-cho, Koi., T. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 122](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n122/mode/1up))

**HATTORI, Ichizo** Gov. of Hoygo, b. '52 in Choshu, and early took to learning English, first at Nagasaki, and then in America from which he returned as B.S. (Rutgers); appointed official of Edu. Dep't with which he was connected for 15 or 16 years as President of the University Prep. Course, Tokyo, manager of the University. Dean of the Law Course. Councillor and Secretary of the Head Office. Was a Japanese Commissioner in the New Orleans Exhibition '83. Tranferred as Gov. of Iwate '91; of Hiroshima '98, then of Nagasaki and lastly the present post. Add. Kencho Off., Kobe. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 122](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n122/mode/1up))

**HAYAKAWA, Senkichiro** General Manager of the Mitsui Bank, b at Kanazawa, '63; grad. Law Course, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '87; Councillor Dep't Finance '91-1900, and was twice dispatched to England by the Matsukata Ministry in connection with the War Indemnity paid by China to Japan and also to raise a foreign loan, and was duly rewarded by his successful service. He resigned the Gov. service '01 to take charge of said Bank, the Mitsui Bank. Add. Nagata-cho, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 122](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n122/mode/1up))

**HAYAKAWA, Tetsuya** b. Okayama, '65; grad. Sapporo Agri. Col. '84; studied law in U.S.A. and Germany; entered the diplomatic service '85 and attached to the Imperial Legation at Seoul until '88; afterward removed to the Consulate at San Francisco, then attaché to the Imperial Legation at Berlin '89-92; Private Secretary to the Minister of Agriculture and Commerce '92-'97. Was appointed Minister Resident '97-'98; Secretary of the Cabinet '98; then Director of the Political Bureau of Foreign Office in the Okuma Cabinet whose reform programme he chiefly undertook. Was Manag. Director of the Nippon Fire Insurance Co., and has taken active part in the recent international economic enterprise of inviting foreign capitalists to invest in Japan, having been principal agent in bringing to successful issue loan negotiations of the Hokkaido Tanko, the Tokyo Elec. Light Co. He is now a prominent figure in business circles and is also a patent attorney of perhaps the widest connection with international patent affairs. Add. Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 77 ([scan leaf 122](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n122/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**HAYASHI, Gonsuke** Minister at Peking b. at Aizu '60; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '89; appointed the same year attaché Foreign Office; eleve Consul at Chefoo '87. at Ninsen '88 promoted full Consul '90, Consul at Shanghai 92, removed to a similar post at London '93 to be appointed six years later Secretary of the Legation. Recalled home he was appointed Director of the Commercial Bureau '98, and to Minister at Seoul the following year. Is a man of strong will and great resolution. Add. Jap. Legation at Peking. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 123](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n123/mode/1up))

**HAYASHI, Tadasu** Viscount, (cr. Baron '95, Visc. '02), Minister of Foreign Affairs, (May '06) b. at Tokyo '50 of a medical family; was one of the students sent for study to England by the Tokugawa Government, to return home in a hurry on the eve of the surrender of the Regency to the Imperial Court; was a captain under Enomoto (now Vis. Adm.) when at the head of a few thousand staunch adherents of the Tokugawa Regency he defended Hakodate against the Imp. Army and upheld cause of the Tokugawa. He was imprisoned two years after the suppression of the rebellion; he was entrusted, when released, by the local Government of Wakayama to adjust its administrative affairs. His career in civil service began in '71 when he was an official in the Kanagawa Kencho; next held posts of Secretary in the Central Government and also in the Household Dep't, to be appointed Governor of Kagawa and Hyogo. We next found him in the Foreign Office as Vice-Minister June '91-May '96 under the late Count Mutsu; was transferred to Minister Plenipotentiary Envoy Extraordinary at Peking '96, then at St. Petersburg '98; to be removed a few months afterward to the Court of St. James which post he continued to occupy with credit. Was created Baron '95, after the Japan-China War, and in 1902 as granted Viscount in consideration of his service in concluding the Treaty of the Anglo-Japanese alliance. He was Jap. Delegate at the Peace Conference held at the Hague '99. The Viscount is something of an author and has written and translated "The Farmer Farmer Patriot" (Sakura Sogoro). Mill's Political Economy and Machiavelli's The Prince. Add. Foreign Office, Tokyo. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 123](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n123/mode/1up))

**HAYASHI, Tomoyuki** Viscount (cr. '87), b. Choshu, '23; Governor of Tokyo early in the era; Member of the Privy Council. Chiefly known in association of his service in the bringing up of the two Imperial Princesses, Fumi and Yasu. Add. Ichibei-cho, Azabu, T. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 123](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n123/mode/1up))

**HAYASHI, Yuzo** M.P., one of the leading politicians and party managers. b. Tosa, '42; Councillor for Kochi-ken '69; Secretary of Foreign Office '73-'74; then entered the political career under Count Itagaki and fought for liberty and popular right; was thrown in prison for ten years on charge of complicity in the Satsuma rebellion. One of the few M.P.s with unbroken record since inauguration of Parl. regime in '90. He sat twice in the Cabinet, chair of Communication in the Okuma-Itagaki Cabinet '98, and of Agriculture and Commerce in the Seiyukwai (4th Ito Ministry). In '03 he seceded from the Seiyukwai and organized the "Liberal Party" as ministerial supporters, but it soon collapsed. Add. Motozono-cho, Koj., T.
inn in Echigo; adopted into the Hibiya family '77 as husband of its daughter and started cotton and yarn shop; and restored its credit after it was declared bankrupt. He has undertaken similar worthy service for other tottering establishments, and is one of the prominent figures in business, and has won greater fortune than any other by the steady rise of shares with the restoration of peace. Add. Hibiya Firm, N., T. — p. 78 ([scan leaf 123](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n123/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**HAYASHIDA, Kametaro** Chief Secretary of the House of Representatives, b. '63 in Kumamoto, and prosecuted study while fighting against adversity. Graduated Law, Imp. Univ., Tokyo. '87; was an official in the Legislative Bureau, Privy Council, and Dep't of Agriculture and Commerce. Transferred to the present post in '97 and has effected various improvement in the administrative routine of the House. Add. Official Res. House of Rep. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 124](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n124/mode/1up))

**HIBI, Osuke** b. at Kurume '62; grad. from the Keio-gijuku '83; a clerk in the Navy; entered the service of the Mushu Factory, Tokyo; entered the Mitsui Ginko about 10 years ago, to be transferred to the Mitsui Dry Goods Dep't a few years after; now Man. Dir. of the Dep't widely known under the title of Mitsukoshi Dry Goods Store. Made a trip through Europe and America '06. Add. Takanawa, T. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 124](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n124/mode/1up))

**HIBIYA, Heizaemon** millionaire. Pres. of Kanegafuchi Cotton Spinning Mill; Man. Dir. Tokyo Gassed Yarn Mill, and Dir. of several other concerns, is a leading cotton yarn merchant in Tokyo. b. 48 in Echigo, son of a small — p. 79 ([scan leaf 124](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n124/mode/1up))

**HIDAKA, Sonojō** Vice-Adm., Com. of Maizuru Adm., b. '49 at Kagoshima, 4th son of S. Miyauchi, retainer of the fief and adopted by Toemon Hidaka of the same clan when 24; midshipman '77, Commander of Cru. Hashidate in the Japan-China War, for which decorated with 4th Order of the Golden Kite; subsequently Dir. Naval Academy and Com. Takeshiki Naval Port; Vice-Adm. 1900 and Com.-in-Chief of Combined Fleet '02, which post he exchanged with Adm. Togo, then Com. Maizuru Adm. on the eve of outbreak of the late war. Holder of 2nd class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun (war honor). Add. Maizuru, Tango. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 124](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n124/mode/1up))

**HIGASHIKUZE, Michiyoshi** Count, (cr. '87), Vice-President of the Privy Council since '91, b. in Kyoto '33: one of the prominent figures as supporters of the Imperial cause in the troubled time preceding the Restoration; was given an important post on the advent of the Imperial Government, as Sangi, the Chief of Hokkaido Colonization Board, etc.; was appointed Vice-President of the House of Peers '90-'91. He is a charm- ing poetaster. Add. Honmura-cho, Az., T. — p. 79 ([scan leaf 124](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n124/mode/1up))

**HIJIKATA, Hisamoto** Count, (cr. '95), b. in Tosa, '33; distinguished himself in the cause of Restoration; was appointed Secretary of the Imp. Household soon after that memorable event of '68; Chief-Secretary in Count Ito's (now Marquis), Cabinet in '85; Minister of Agriculture and Commerce July '87-Sept. '87, and Minister of the Imperial Household from September, '87 to November '98. Add. Hayashi-cho, Koi., T. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 125](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n125/mode/1up))

**HIRAI, Seijiro** Kogaku-hakushi, Dir.-Gen. of Gov. Rlys., b. '55 at Kanazawa. Was despatched by Gov. to study civil eng. at Troy, U.S.A. '75, and grad. '77. Stayed in America some years after to study practical side. Was engaged as Rly. Eng. by Hok. Gov. on his return '80, then Chief Eng. of Tanko Rly. '94, when he entered Gov. Rly's in which he steadily rose till he was appointed to the present post '04. Add. Shiodome, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 125](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n125/mode/1up))

**HIRANUMA, Kiichiro** Dir. Civil and Criminal Affairs Bureau, b. '65 in Tsuyama; graduated Law, Imp. Cniv., Tokyo, '88, and at once appointed on the bench, having filled chair of sectional chief '95 in the Tokyo Appeal Court; next appointed Public Procurator of the same court, and finally transferred to the present post '06. Add. Wakamiya-cho, U., Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 125](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n125/mode/1up))

**HIRAOKA, Ki** Pioneer of rolling-stock makers in Japan, Vice-Pres. Osaka Rolling-Stock Co. b. in Tokyo, went over to U.S.A. when he was a boy of 16 and so powerfully impressed with the importance of r'ly. business as factor of civilisation, that he at once decided to learn the business of making rolling-stock and was allowed to enter some factory in New York as apprentice; went through the regular stages, and returned home about '80, to be appointed at once as overseer of the works at Shimbashi. Left the service about '90 and started the Hiraoka Rolling-Stock Works at Honjo, Tokyo, which proved an immense success as this was the only private factory of the kind then in Japan. When Viscount Inouye, his former chief at teh Government Railways, with a number of capitalists started the Osaka Rolling-Stock Co. the subject of the sketch was induced after some years to wind up his own business and to unite it with the Osaka factory. Add. Nagata-cho, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 125](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n125/mode/1up))

**HIRATA, Tosuke** Baron, (cr. '02) Mem. House of Peers, b. '49 in Yamagata-ken; filled various posts in civil office since '70, attaining the post of Minister of Agriculture and Commerce June 1901-July '03. Is a trusted follower of Marshal Marquis Yamagata, and is actively engaged in preaching the importance of co-operation for promoting communal prosperity. Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 125](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n125/mode/1up))

**HIRAYAMA, Tojiro** Cap. (Imp. Navy ret.), Pres. Nautical Col. Tokyo; b. in Tokushima '52; entered the Naval Acad. which he grad. '73. and was Capt. of the scouting vessel Yayeyama on the occasion of the Japan-China War but was to leave the service '96 owning to the protest of England for his search of a British vessel at that time; removed the same year to the present post. Add. Nautical Co., Tokyo. Add. Yarai, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 80 ([scan leaf 125](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n125/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**HIROI, Isamu** Prof. Engineering, Imp. Univ., Tokyo; b. '62 in Kochi; graduated Sapporo Agr. College, class '81; proceeded to America, Dec. '83, and was employe of the Mississippi River Work Office and some other engineering offices '84-'87; ordered by the Hokkaido Government to study civil engineering at Karlsruhe and Stutgart '87-'89, and was granted C.E., returning home '89. From that year till 1900 when he was transferred to the present post, he was Prof. at the alma mater and Eng. to the Hokkaido Government, and had charge of the harbor works of Hakodate (completed) and Otaru. Add. Kaga-cho, Ichigaya, T. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 126](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n126/mode/1up))

**HIROMI, Jisaburo** "highest tax" Member of the House of Peers for Osaka; b. in Kaga, '54: promoted the Japan Marine Insurance Co. and appointed its President, and is one of the largest shipowners in Japan. Add. Yedo-bori, Osaka. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 126](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n126/mode/1up))

**HIROTSU, Ryuro** one of the leading novelists (Thomas Hardy of Japan). b. in Kurume '60; first studied Medicine; then clerk of the Osaka Chamber of Com. and next a clerk in Dep't of Agr. and Com. About '87 his first story sent to a Tokyo daily won wide fame and he resigned Gov. service to devote himself to literature. Has since produced about twenty novels and novelettes, his strength lying in depicting tragic side of womens' character. Of the Three Celebrities, Roban, Koyo and Ryuro, the first has of late devoting his time more for study than writing, the second is dead, and Ryuro alone almost remains on the field. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 126](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n126/mode/1up))

**HOMMA, Mitsuyoshi** the house of celebrated "farmer King of Japan" dating over 150 years back, b. 37 at his ancestral seat in Sakata, Dewa. Add. Sakata, Yamagata-ken. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 126](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n126/mode/1up))

**HONDA, Yo-ichi, D.D** President of Tokyo Aoyama Gakuin (a Christian College), a leading educationalist and Christian evangelist in Japan, born '48 at Hirosaki; was converted into a Christian when he was 24. He bade adieu '83 to politics to devote himself to Christian work. He visited Europe and America a number of times either for prosecution of studies or to attend an international Christian association as representative of Japan, last at the World's Y.M.C.A. union held at Paris '05. Add. Aoyama Gakuin, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 126](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n126/mode/1up))

**HOSOKAWA, Junjiro, Baron, (cr. '00)** scholar of great erudition, b. in Tosa, '34; has been Privy Councillor since '93; Vice-President of the House of Peers '91-'93; was the President of the Peers' Girls School, and Chief of the Secretariate Bureau of the Imp. Household; wrote a number of works of high literary merit. Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 126](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n126/mode/1up))

**HOZUMI, Nobushige, Hogaku-hakushi** Professor of Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. b. in Iyo, '55; grad. Middle Temple, England, '76; afterwards studied at Berlin University '81; Member of the House of Peers '90-92, which post he resigned to devote himself to investigation and study. Among his works the "Codes," the "Inkyo" (retirement from active life by transferring the right of family head to the heir), the "Go-nin-gumi" (sys- tem of mutual help among neighbourhood) may be mentioned. Add. Haraikata-machi, U., T. '82. Mrs. Ibuka is B.A. of a female Seminary, U.S.A. Add. Meiji Gakuin, Sb., T. — p. 81 ([scan leaf 126](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n126/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**HOZUMI, Yatsuka** Hogaku-hakushi, brother of the above, born in Iyo, '60; pursued science of constitution and administrative politics in Germany '88; is the Professor in his special line, Imp. Tokyo Univ. since '89, also Secretary of the Privy Council from '91; appointed Member of the House of Peers in '99. Add. Hara-machi, Koi., T. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 127](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n127/mode/1up))

**IBARAGI, Isho** Baron and Lieut.-Gen. (ret.), b. '49 in Wakayama and took part in the civil war of the Restoration and was commissioned Major '72 as soon as the Army was organized on a new model; Maj.-Gen. 90 and was Mil. Gov. of Kinchow and Chief of Civil Adm. Dir. of Commander-in-Chief's Office of the Districts of Occupation in the Japan-China War. He was raised to Peerage with the rank of Baron after the War; was Commander of the 4th Depot Div. during the late War. Holder of 2nd class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun (war honor). — p. 82 ([scan leaf 127](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n127/mode/1up))

**IBUKA, Kajinosukê, M.A** President of the Meiji Gakuin, b. in Aizu and took part when he was only 15 in the defense of Aizu against the Imperial troops, to be released on account of age; studied at Dr. Brown's School in Yokohama and was converted; then crossed over to U.S.A. where he graduated the Course of Theology at the Union Seminary. Attended the World's Students Y.M.C.A. Conference held in U.S.A. several years ago. Has been connected with the present institution since its founding — p. 82 ([scan leaf 127](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n127/mode/1up))

**ICHIHARA, Morihiro** Director of First Bank and its Agent in Korea and ex-Mayor of Yokohama, b. at Kumamoto, '58, graduated the late Dr. Niejima's school at Kyoto, Doshi-sha, and for several years Christian Pastor; entered the Bank of Japan about 11 years ago, to enter the 1st Bank which appointed him Manager of the Bank's Yokohama branch. He resigned it to be elected Mayor of Yokohama, '02; re-entered the 1st Bank '06. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 127](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n127/mode/1up))

**ICHIJI, Kosuke** Lieut.-General (Artillery), b. '54 in Satsuma; entered Army in '71, appointed a member of the General Staff; was on the Staff of General Oyama in the Japan-China War; afterward Military Attaché to the Japanese Legation at London and Seoul; distinguished himself in the recent war as chief staff of officers under Gen. Nogi. Holder of 2nd class Golden Kite and of Rising Sun (war honor). Add. Aoyama. Tokyo. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 127](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n127/mode/1up))

**ICHIKI, Kitokuro** Hogaku-hakushi, Prof. of Law at the Imp. Tokyo Univ., b. Shizuoka, '65; studied the science of constitution, administrative politics, etc. in Germany '90-'93; Chief Councillor of the Home Office, Professor of the Tokyo University '94; Director of the Legislative Bureau '02-'06; nominated Member of the House of Peers in 1900. Add. Wakamiya-cho, U., T. — p. 82 ([scan leaf 127](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n127/mode/1up))

**ICHINOHE, Hyoê** Maj.-Gen.; noted for his stubbon resistance in holding a position secured at Panlungsh in the 1st general assault delivered on Port Arthur ('04); b. '55 in Hiroshima: Sub.-Lieut. '77; Capt. '83; Major '88; Lieut.-Col. 94; took part in the Japan-China War at the head of a Reg. and was rewarded with 4th order of Golden Kite; Col. '97; Chief Staff of 6th Div., '98;Maj.-Gen. '02. Holder of 2nd class Golden Kite, and of Rising Sun (war honor). — p. 82 ([scan leaf 127](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n127/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**IIDA, Shunsuke** Lieut.-Gen. (ret), b. '46 in Yamaguchi; Sub.-Lieut. '73, Major '83, Lieut.-Col. '90 and staff officer of 2nd Div., Col. '94, Maj.-Gen. '98, Lieut.-Gen. '03. In the late war he fought under Gen. Nogi as Com. of the 1st Div., and was decorated with the 2nd class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun (war honor.). — p. 83 ([scan leaf 128](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n128/mode/1up))

**IJUIN, Goro** Vice-Admiral, and Com. of the Standing Squadron b. '52 in Satsuma; took part in the Restoration War while he was still a minor; entered the Naval Academy '71; despatched to England for study '78 and was on board the Triumph as cadet '79-'82; next graduated '83 the Greenwich Academy receiving diploma on Torpedo Course; Lieutenant '85, returned home '86 on board the Naniwa which was just completed in England; member of Staff at the Naval Staff Board '85-'89; brought home from England Chiyoda as Vice-Commander '89-'91; attached to the Naval Staff Board '92-'95; meanwhile promoted to Captain and was on board the Saikyo Maru in the Battle of Yellow Sea '95; rewarded with 4th class Golden Kite and of Rising Sun; in charge of duty of Vice-Chief of Naval Staff '98; Rear-Adm. '99; invented the Ijuin fuse '00 and was granted 3rd class Rising Sun and Y. 200; with 2nd class Rising Sun and Y. 2,500 in connection with the Boxer Trouble; represented Japan with Asama and Takasago in the Coronation Ceremony of King Edward; Vice-Adm. '03 to be appointed Vice-Chief of Naval Staff; transferred to command of 2nd Standing Squadron Nov. '06; represented Japan in James Town Exhibition U.S.A. April '07 with Tsukuba and Chitose. Granted 1st class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun for his service in the late War. Add. Nagata-Cho, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 128](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n128/mode/1up))

**IKEBE, Kichitaro** journalist and chief editor of the Tokyo Asahi, born at Kumamoto '61, a son of Kichijuro Ikebe, who at the head of Kumamoto contingent joined the rebellion raised by the elder Saigo's followers in '77. The young Ikebe pursued study for several years in France and after his return home '90 entered journalism, and for several years stayed in North-China as special correspondent of the Osaka Asahi. Add. Yanagi-cho, Ichigaya, T. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 128](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n128/mode/1up))

**IKEDA, Kensai** Dr., Baron, (cr. '99), b. '41 in Echigo; pursued his medical study in Germany '70-'76; appointed Surgeon-Colonel in '76; chief physician-in-ordinary at the Court. Add. Surugadai, Kanda, T. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 128](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n128/mode/1up))

**IKEDA, Kenzo** banker, b. in Tajima, '56; Managing Director of the "Dai Hyaku Ginko" (One Hundredth Bank) since '83; chairman of the Japan Commercial Association; reputed as being one of the most wide-awake business-men. Add. Tsukiji, Tokyo. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 128](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n128/mode/1up))

**INAGAKI, Manjiro** Minister Plenip'y at Madrid, b. at Hirado '61; entered the Tokyo Univ. '82 which he did not graduate; entered Cambridge, England, '86 which he graduated (B.A.) '89; was then lecturer at the Noble's School and the Tokyo Higher Commercial School, after which he took to his lecture-tour through principal districts, "preaching" on the catchy sub-ject, the "Oriental Policy." In '97 appointed to Minister Resident accredited at Bangkok, and transferred to be Minister to Spain and Portugal, '07. He is an author of some political works. — p. 83 ([scan leaf 128](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n128/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**INOGUCHI, Seigo** Maj.-Gen., Pres. Mil. Staff Col. and Mem. General Staff Board, b. '55 in Shizuoka-ken; entered Mil. Academy '75; Sub.-Lieut. Artillery '79; student of Mil. Staff Col. '84; despatched to Germany to study tactics '87-'90; instructor at Mil. Staff Col. on returning home and Major in '91; Staff Office of 2nd Army in the Japan-China War and was rewarded with 4th Order Golden Kite; Lieut.-Col. '95; Sectional Chief at War Office '01; Maj.-Gen. '02 and transferred to the present post. 2nd class Golden Kite and of Rising Sun (war honor.) Add. Yodobashi, near Tokyo. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 129](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n129/mode/1up))

**INOUYE, Hikaru** Com. 4th Army (Osaka) Div.; b. '51 in Yamaguchi, 2nd son of a samurai of the clan and adopted in childhood into the present family, also of the same clan; Capt. in '71. Major in '76 and took part in the civil war of Satsuma; Lieut.-Col. '85; Col. '88; Chief Staff to 3rd Division '89; dispatched abroad '94; was recalled in a hurry on the eve of the Japan-China War when as Major-Gen. and Chief Staff to the 2nd Army he took distinguished part and was rewarded with 3rd order of Golden Kite; Chief Staff of Military Inspection Board '96; Lieut.-Gen. '99 as Com. of Kokura Div. which he led under Gen. Kuroki in the Russo-Japanese War; was transferred to the present command in '06.. Was granted 2nd class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun (war honor.) — p. 84 ([scan leaf 129](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n129/mode/1up))

**INOUYE, Jukichi** Sec. Foreign Office but more widely known as one of the best Japanese writers of English; b. '62 in Tokushima; proceeded to England in '73. Finished the course at Rugby 81 and then the course of metallurgy at Royal School of Mines. returning home '83. Except for only a few months he has never followed this speciality, having devoted himself to his favorite pursuit of English. Was Prof. of English at the Tokyo High School till '93, Japanese editor on the staff of the Japan Gazette Secretary of Legataion at Brussels and Washington, remaining at the head office since '02, being at the same time Prof. at the Higher Normal School. Add. Ichiban-cho, Koj., T. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 129](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n129/mode/1up))

**INOUYE, Kakugoro** M.P., b. in Hiroshima, '59; stayed in U.S.A. for some years; started a paper at Seoul and was appointed adviser to the Korean Government in 1884; gallantly cut his way when in that year the Japanese Legation was assaulted by the Korean mob. Since 1890 he has represented Hiroshima-ken in the House of Representatives, where his sarcastic eloquence attracts wide attention: is the Managing Director of the Hokkaido Coal & Steamship Co. and was Vice-Pres. of the Tokyo Chamber of Commerce. Add. Mita, Tokyo. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 129](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n129/mode/1up))

**INOUYE, Kaoru** Count, (cr. '84), one of the elder statesmen, b. '35 in Choshu; played a distinguished part in '63 when the clan of Choshu defended itself against the attack of the overwhelming host of the Tokugawa. With his bosom friend Marquis Ito he secretly visited England in '63, to return in a hurry on learning that the allied Squadron of the Powers was going to bombard Shimonoseki. He and Ito were regarded as renegades by the fellow clansmen who blessed with ignorance scorned at the idea of opening country to hold intercourse with the "red-haired Barbarians." Inouye was waylaid by a set of these men, was terribly wounded and narrowly escaped death. Soon after the consummation of the Restoration, the future Count was appointed Vice-Minister of Finance which he resigned for a short while in 1873, soon to re-enter the Government service; was dispatched '74 as Vice-Plenipotentiary with the late Count Kuroda, Chief Plenipotentiary, to Korea to negotiate with that Government about the wanton firing on our warship by the Korean fort when she called at Kokwa island to get supply of water. Subsequently down to 1898 when he made up his mind to retire from active life, he occupied the chairs of the Home Affairs, Foreign Affairs, Finance, and Agriculture and acting Premier in addition to chair of Home Office when Marquis Ito, then Premier, met with an accident and was confined in bed while Parliament was sitting; was also Minister to Korea and Adviser to the Court sent soon after the close of the Japan-China War, though his excessive interference did not make his mission a success. On the outbreak of the Russo-Japanese war the Count with the other elder statesmen was ordered to attend to all important council and specially to render advice to the Minister of Finance, and even at present he is a "Guardian" of the Treasury. He is reputed to be a statesman of vigorous intellect, but to laack breadth of views; possesses a shrewd business talent and is a great virtuos in arts, he possessing perhaps the best collection of Sesshu. He has no male issue, all his children being girls. The eldest is the wife of Hon. Inouye, Japanese Amb. Berlin, who is the Count's adopted heir, the second is the wife of Senator Tsutsuki, Chief Secretary of the Privy Council and Member of the House of Peers, and the third the wife of Mr. Fujita, Member of the House of Peers. (Vide Chap. "Senior Statesmen.") Add. Uchidayama, Az., T. — p. 84 ([scan leaf 129](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n129/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**INOUYE, Katsunosuke** Japanese Amb. at Berlin, b. in Choshu, '60; was on the service of the Nippon Ginko before he got appointment at Foreign Office in '85, to be sent to Berlin the following year as secretary of Legation there. Was back to the Foreign Office as secretary and councillor '92, and in '98 he was appointed Envoy Extraordinary and Minister Plenipotentiary, promoted to Amb. '06. Granted 1st class Rising Sun (war honor). — p. 85 ([scan leaf 130](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n130/mode/1up))

**INOUYE, Ryuko** Admiral. Baron, (cr. '87). b. 45 in Satsuma; Captain '82; Vice-Admiral '92; Admiral '98; Commander-in-Chief of the Yokosuka Admiral- ty, for several years, and removed to post of Mem. of the Supreme Military Council, Dec. '05. Holds 2nd class Golden Kite. Add. Hiro-o, Azabu, T. — p. 85 ([scan leaf 130](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n130/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**INOUYE, Teijiro** greatest match exp. of Japan, b. in Tajima '53, son of an obscure county doctor; left home '74 to seek fortune. After hard fighting against adversity at Tokyo and Osaka, he at last succeeded in placing his match-making business on sound basis. He was granted the Blue Ribbon medal in '02 in recognition of important improvements he made in the export business of matches. Add. Oaska Koyekisha, Osaka. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 131](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n131/mode/1up))

**INOUYE, Tetsujiro** Bungakuhakushi, b. '55; Fukuoka-ken. pursued philosophial study in Germany and France '84-'90; the Dean and Professor College of Literature of the Imperial Tokyo Univ. since '90. Among his numerous works may be mentioned the Life of Buddha, the Oriental Philosophy, etc. Add. Omote-cho, Koi., T. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 131](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n131/mode/1up))

**INOUYE, Tsuyoshi** Viscount, (cr. '87), Member of the House of Peers, born '43 in Choshu; held for many years the post Director-General of Government Railways, which he resigned in 1887; lost greater part of his fortune through failure of the Oriental Bank, Yokohama; is now president of the Rolling-stock Co., Osaka. Add. Enokisaka. Ak., T. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 131](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n131/mode/1up))

**INUKAI, Ki** M.P., a leader of the Progressive Party, and a M.P. of unbroken record, born at Okayama '55; early began political career; was editor of the "Hochi Shinbun" till the opening of the National Diet '90; — p. 86 ([scan leaf 131](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n131/mode/1up))

**IRISAWA, Tatsukichi** Dr., Igakuhakushi, was b. in Echigo; grad. Berlin Univ. in '94; since '95 Prof. Med. Col., Imp. Tokyo Univ.; also attends to his additional function as physician-in-ordinary to the Court. Add. Hisamatsu-cho, N., T. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 131](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n131/mode/1up))

**ISEJI, Kosei** Lieut.-Gen. (ret.), b. '48 in Kagoshima; Major '82. Col. '90, and held post of chief staff officer to 1st (Tokyo) Div. when it was commanded by the late Gen. Yamaji in the Japan-China War, was rewarded with 4th Golden Kite for the service. Lieut.-Gen. '00 and Com. 6th Division to be placed on retired list '02, and filled post of Com. of Depot Body Guards Div. during the late war for which he was rewarded with Grand Cordon of Rising Sun. — p. 86 ([scan leaf 131](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n131/mode/1up))
