Started career as clerk in Foreign Office, to be attached to the Legation at Paris and other places; was ordered to prosecute study on official expense at Brussel Univ. '86 which finished '88. Appointed attache '93; 3rd Sec. Legation at Holland and Paris '95; Per. Sec. to Foreign Min. '97 combining that to Min. Agr. and Com.; 2nd Sec. at London '98; promoted to 1st Sec. the same year at the same post of duty; Sec. at home '02. Add. Sendai-zaka, Az., Tokyo. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n154/mode/1up))

**MATSUMOTO, Jutaro** once a great power in business circle of Osaka, but subsequently lost this position owing to that stumbling of the 130th Bank, Osaka, of which he was President. He was b. '44 in Tamba; amassed a fortune by his shrewd business foresight and promoted the 130th Bank to be elected its President. Since then till his retirement from active business '04 he had been connected with almost all new important enterprises started in Osaka. Add. Higashi-ku, Osaka. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n154/mode/1up))

**MATSUNAGA, Masatoshi** holder of 2nd class Golden Kite and of Rising Sun (war honor), Leut.-Gen. and Com. 3rd (Nagoya) Div., b. in Kumamoto '51; Sub.-Lieut. '76; was rewarded wth 4th Order of Golden Kite for his service in the Japan-China War; Maj.-Gen. '00; fought in the late War first as Com. of a Brigade of the 2nd Div. (Sendai) and subsequently promoted to the present post. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n154/mode/1up))

**MATSUO, Shinzen** Governor of the Bank of Japan, Member of the House of Peers, b. '43 at Uwajima; was Director of the Financial Bureau of the Treasury for a long while, till in '03 he was nominated to the present post. Add. Shinsaka-machi, Ak., T. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n154/mode/1up))

**MATSUOKA, Koki** Minister of Ari. and Com. and Mem. House of Peers, b. '46 at Tokushima; appointed President of the Tokyo Appeal Court '90; Procurator-General '92. He was twice the Vice-Minister of Home Affairs in '94 and '98, then removed to President of the Adm Litigation Court till Jan. '06 when he entered the Cabinet. He is one of the staunch followers of Marquis Yamagata. Add. Fujimi-cho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n154/mode/1up))

**MATSUZAKI, Kuranosuke** Dir. Higher Com. School b. '62; grad. Univ. '85; studied further in France and Germany; Prof. at both Agr. Col. and Law Col. of the Univ., appointed to present post '02. Add. Suyedo-cho, Koi., T. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n154/mode/1up))

**MEGATA, Tanetaro** Financial Adviser to Korean Government, b. '51 in Tokyo, one of the first Japanese who grad. Harvard, and for about 25 years ending '04 when he was engaged by the Korean Gov. Mr. Megata continued in the service of the Finance Dep't where he occupied the post of one Bureau Director or another. Holds 2nd class Rising Sun. Add. Seoul, Korea. — p. 109 ([scan leaf 154](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n154/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**MINAKAMI, Hiromi** Mayor of Kobe, b. '63 at Kumamoto; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. '88; appointed Councillor of Legislative Bureau and dispatched to Europe to investigate Parliamentary system; appointed Sec. House of Representatives, of Nagasaki Local Office, Customs House at Kobe and that at Yokohama in turn which last he resigned '05 to take up the present post. Add. Kobe-Shiyaku-sho, Kobe. — p. 110 ([scan leaf 155](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n155/mode/1up))

**MINOBE, Shunkichi** Pres. Hokkaido Colonization Bank, b. '68 at Takasago, Harima; grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ. 93; held posts of private Secretary, Sectional chief both at the Dep't Agri. and Com. and the Treasury; elected to the present post '03. Add. Hok. Col. Bk., Sapporo. — p. 110 ([scan leaf 155](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n155/mode/1up))

**MINOURA, Katsundo** Vice-President of the House of Representatives. M.P. of unbroken record, b. '54 in Bungo, acts for a long while as manager of Hochi. He began political life under Count Okuma; twice entered the Government service, first as Director of the now abolished Commercial Bureau of the Dep't of Agri're and Com. in '97 and in '98 as Vice-Minister of Communicatiton. Add. Higashi-goken-cho, U., T. — p. 110 ([scan leaf 155](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n155/mode/1up))

**MISHIMA, Hatasu** tutor of Chinese classics to the Crown Prince. b. in Bitchu '30, and held a post in the Justice Dep't as Public Procurator of the Supreme Court. Was also a Prof. in the Imp. Tokyo Univ. Add. Gobancho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 110 ([scan leaf 155](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n155/mode/1up))

**MISU, Sotaro** holder of 2nd class Golden Kite and of Rising Sun (war honor), Vice-Adm., Deputy Chief of the Naval Staff, b. at Hikone '54; Captain at the time of the Japan-China War; Rear-Adm. '01, and Director of the Personnel Bureau; during the late War he was Com. of the 2nd Squad. under Vice-Adm. Kamimura and wounded in one eye in the memorable Battle of the Japan Sea; promoted to Vice-Admiral early '05; appointed Chief Naval Education Board, transferred to the post of Commander of Naval Station, Port Arthur, Feb. '06, and finally to the present post, Nov. same year. Add. Omote-cho, Koi., T. — p. 110 ([scan leaf 155](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n155/mode/1up))

**MITSUHASHI, Nobukata** Mayor of Yokohama, b. '56; clerk in the Foreign Office '79; transferred 2 years later to the Kanagawa and Secretary of the Foreign Office '96; Minister Resident 1900, and accredited to The Hague, which he resigned in '06 to accept the present post. Add. Yokohama City Office. — p. 110 ([scan leaf 155](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n155/mode/1up))

**MITSUI, family, The** Is one of the oldest millionaire families and the most noted hereditary houses of "business kings" in Japan, managing the big family concerns somewhat after a fashion of constitutional monarchy, for the eleven heads of the main stock and scions of the family are individually insignificant and only acquire importance as proprietors of different concerns the control of which is left in the hands of "executive officials." These establishments are the Mitsui Bank (manager Mr. Senkichiro Hayakawa), Mitsui Bussan Kaisha (trading dep't) and Mitsui Kozan Kaisha (mining dep't); the second under Mr. Senjiro Watanabe, and the third under Mr. Takuma Dan. They are backed with registered nominal capitals of 8½ mil. yen and reserve funds amounting to 17 millions. The head of the main stock, Hachiroemon Mitsui, was created a Baron 1900 in consideration of valuable services rendered by the family to cause of public interest from former time. Add. (of the Baron) Bancho, Koj., T. — p. 110 ([scan leaf 155](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n155/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**MITSUKURI, brothers** family celebrated in the field of science. The late Rinsho Mitsukuri, eldest of the four, was one of the most noted jurists of the time and was made a Baron shortly before death. Of the three remaining brothers the eldest is Baron Kikuchi (which see) adopted by a relative of the house; the second is Prof. K. Mitsukuri, of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. who grad. Yale and Cambridge, biology being his special line, and he is connected with the Univ. since his return '85. The youngest, Prof. G. Mitsukuri, studied history in Germany, and is a Prof. of the Col. of Lit. of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. Add. Tokyo Imp. Univ. — p. 111 ([scan leaf 156](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n156/mode/1up))

**MIURA, Goro** Lieut.-Gen. (retired), Visc. (cr. '84), b. '46 in Choshu; Commander of the Hiroshima Army Division '76; fought in the Civil War of '77; promoted Lieut.-General '79; afterward Commander of the Tokyo Army Division, &c.; retired from military service '88. It was while he was at Seoul as Japanese Minister '95 that the memorable tragedy of the assassination of the Queen took place; he was arrested on suspicion of implicity, but the suspicion was cleared on examination and soon set free. He is an earnest votarist of the Zen doctrines of Buddhism. Add. Naka-Tomisaka, Koi., T. — p. 111 ([scan leaf 156](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n156/mode/1up))

**MIURA, Taisuke** ex-Pres. of lately nationalized Kobu R'ly, b. in Choshu '56, brother of Vis't. Aoki, U.S.A. Ambassador; went his brother to Berlin accompanied studied agriculture and wine brewing in Germany '74-'79; commenced farming on the field purchased by his brother at Koganehara, Chiba, which however was not a success; was engaged '87-'89 on the reclaimed foreshore at Kojima, Osaka, undertaken by the Fujita family; was elected Pres. Kobu R'ly, (elec. '90). Add. Surugadai, Tokyo. — p. 111 ([scan leaf 156](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n156/mode/1up))

**MIURA, Yasushi** Mem. House of Peers, born Aug. '28 in Ehimeken; took some distinguished part at the time of the Restoration; Gov. of Tokyo '93-'94. He is Lord-in-waiting of the Kinkei Hall. Add. Kobiki-cho, Tokyo. — p. 111 ([scan leaf 156](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n156/mode/1up))

**MIWADA, Masako** was b. '44 at Kyoto, one of the few lady educationalists of great renown. After she was bereft of her husband by death in '78, she took to learning with undivided attention, attaining great eminence in Japanese and Chinese, and enabling her to devote herself to the work of education. By thrift and diligence she amassed enough to build a new school-house and this cherished wish was carried out a few years ago. Add. Gobancho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 111 ([scan leaf 156](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n156/mode/1up))

**MIYABE, Hisashi** Pres. Tokyo Woolen Mill at Oji, b. '44 in Mito; was confined for about five years by his rival faction in the clan, and was released when he was 23. Then after a short career in civil service, he entered business and was connected with the Kawasaki and the Hundredth Banks, and the Tokyo Rice Exchange; finally he started the present Woolen Factory '87. He was twice confined on ungrounded suspicion of complicity in peculation either by his fellow Directors or by clerks. Add. Oji Woolen Mill, Tokyo. — p. 111 ([scan leaf 156](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n156/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**MIYABE, Kingo** Rigaku-hakushi, Prof. of the Sapporo Agr'ture Col., b. in Tokyo '59; grad. the College '81, and was soon sent to U.S.A. to study botany under Asa Gray. Returned home '87 with D. Sc. from Harvard; is one of the foremost specialists in Japan in his line. Add. Sapporo, Hokkaido. — p. 112 ([scan leaf 157](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n157/mode/1up))

**MIYAGAWA, Kozan** founder of the Makuzu Porcelain Ware and Commissioner to the Imp. Household, b. '43 in Kyoto, 3rd son of the family of master ceramist; removed to Yokohama '70 at the invitation of a purveyor to the Lord of Satsuma who conceived an idea to produce porcelains for export. Kozan's great feat was the discovery of a simple process of painting the unglazed biscuit, and his wares have frequently won highest prizes both at domestic and foreign exhibitions; appointed Art Com. to the Imp. Household '96. Add. Nishi-Ota, Yokohama. — p. 112 ([scan leaf 157](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n157/mode/1up))

**MIYAKE, Shu** Dr., Igaku-hakushi, Mem. House of Peers, b. '43 at Tokyo; is the Dean of the Col. of Med. of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. Add. Takehaya-cho, Koi., T. — p. 112 ([scan leaf 157](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n157/mode/1up))

**MIYAKE, Yujiro** Bungaku-hakushi, author and journalist, b. '60 at Kanazawa, and grad. Philosophy — p. 112 ([scan leaf 157](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n157/mode/1up))

**MIZUNO, Rentaro** Dir. of Shinto Affairs Bureau, Home Office, b. '68 in Akita;—graduated Law, Imp. Univ., '92; official of Dep't of Agr. and Com. '93; removed to Home Office '95 as Councillor and Personal Secretary; sent abroad '96 to investigate copyright affairs and attended as Japanese delegate the World's Conference for the Protection of Right of Industrial Property held at Brussels; returned home '98 and framed the draft of the Copyright Law. Elevated to the present post '04. He once held a chair as additional duty at the Imp. University. Add. Uchisaiwai-cho, Koj., T. — p. 112 ([scan leaf 157](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n157/mode/1up))

**MIZUNO, Toshikata** b. '65 in Tokyo; began studying painting when 15 under Yoshitoshi, master Ukiyo painter of the time, but had to give up owing to ill-health; next learned painting for decorating unglazed porcelain and at 20 made a chief painter of a certain Satsuma Yaki factory. Again returned to pursue the study of pure art under his old master, and also the southern School of Chinese painting, but with the death of his two masters he next learned under Watanabe Shotei the Yosai style of painting, resulting in developing a special style of Ukiyoye. (See Chap. Ukiyoyê.) Add. Kiyomizu-cho, Shitaya, T. official career, having been connected with military surgery since he came back '88 from Germany where he had stayed about four years to complete his medical studies; is at present Surgeon-Gen. of the Tokyo Division. Was granted 3rd class Golden Kite and 2nd class Rising Sun (war honor). Add. Sendagi, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 112 ([scan leaf 157](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n157/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**MIYAOKA, Tsunejiro** Councillor of the Japanese Embassy, U.S.A.; b. '67 in Tokyo, second son of Mr. K. Takenaka, but adopted into the present family; grad. Law, Imp. Univ. (Tokyo) '87, to be appointed soon after attaché at the Foreign Office; Legation Sec. and Coun. of Foreign Office '90; Sec. Japanese Legation, England, 92; transferred to a similar post the following year in Berlin; 1st Sec. '96; Coun. and Sec. at head office '00 to be raised to Minister Resident (unattached) the same year. He was commissioned to represent Japan in the House Tax Arbitration at the Hague. — p. 113 ([scan leaf 158](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n158/mode/1up))

**MIYOSHI, Taizo** Mem. House of Peers since '98, lawyer, b. '45: entered civil service while young. transferred to the Bench in which he won distinction till he rose to the post of Vice-Minister of Justice, President of the Court of Cassation which resigned in '97. Add. Sendagaya Onden, Tokyo. — p. 113 ([scan leaf 158](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n158/mode/1up))

**MORI, Kwai-nan** b. '63 at Nagoya; is a writer of Chinese poetry and is patronized on that account by Marquis Ito whose verses are said to receive finishing touch from his pen. Add. Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 113 ([scan leaf 158](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n158/mode/1up))

**MORI, Rintaro** Dr. Igaku-hakushi, Sur. Gen., b. in Iwami '60; is bably more widely known as literateur, chiefly as translator of many German classics, and writes a highly finished style. He is equally famous in his — p. 113 ([scan leaf 158](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n158/mode/1up))

**MORIMOTO, Shun** M.P. and a well-known politician identified with the Seiyukai, b. in Tajima '56; once held a clerical post in the Treasury, but soon resigned and entered active politics and also journalism in both of which his knowledge in finance did him a good turn; was appointed Per. Sec. to the Min. of Finance in the Kenseito Coalition Cabinet; undertook a tour of inspecting economic affairs in Europe and America '00-'02, and has been returned to the House since that time. Has written articles on the virtue of drinking. Add. Shinmachi, Ak., Tokyo. — p. 113 ([scan leaf 158](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n158/mode/1up))

**MORIMURA, Ichizaemon** b. Oct. '39 in Tokyo; is engaged in export and import business for two decades and more, maintaining a branch store at New York and other places; runs the Morimura Bank. Cap. ¥500,000. and was a director of the Bank of Japan. Add. Takanawa, Shiba, Tokyo. — p. 113 ([scan leaf 158](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n158/mode/1up))

**MORITA, Mokichi** Dir. Com. and Ind. Bureau, Dep't Agr. and Com. b. '66 in Hyogo-ken; grad. Law. Imp. Univ., '90, and held posts at prefectural offices and also Central Government till appointed Director of Sanitary Bureau '02, to be transferred to the present office the following year. Has travelled through Europe and America twice on official business. Add. Daimachi Ak. T. — p. 113 ([scan leaf 158](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n158/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**MOTODA, Hajime** M.P. of unbroken record, lawyer, b. '58 in Oita; was a leader of the National Association which was organized in '92 by the lated Marquis Saigo and Viscount Shinagawa as organ of the clan statesmen; was one of those who joined Marquis Ito's "model party" in 1900; was Vice-President of the House '98-1902. Add. 8, Kioi-cho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 114 ([scan leaf 159](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n159/mode/1up))

**MOTODA, Sakunoshin** Ph. D. (Philadelphia), Dir. Rikkyo Daigaku, a prominent Christian teacher, b. 62, in Kurumê; crossed over to America '86 and remained there till '96, meanwhile studying Divinity, Philosophy etc. at Kennyou, Buckslay and Philadelphia, and also attending to religious work. He attended the grand conference of Y.M.C.A. held at Boston '01 as representative of Japan and last year travelled throuhg India. Add. Nishi Okubo, near Tokyo. — p. 114 ([scan leaf 159](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n159/mode/1up))

**MOTORA, Yujiro** Prof. of Imp. Univ., Tokyo; b. '58 in Sanda; studied at the Doshisha '75-'84; proceeded to America and studied Philosophy and Mental Science at Boston and John's Hopkin's Universities '84-'88, receiving Ph. D. from the latter. Appointed to the present post on returning home. Add. Nishikata-machi Hg. T. — p. 114 ([scan leaf 159](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n159/mode/1up))

**MOTOYAMA, Hak-un** sculptor, b. '71 in Kochi, studied the art under Prof. Takamura Ko-un and graduated the Government Fine Art Academy '95 and filled for a short while the chair of assist teacher at the alma mater and also taught at some places. Gave up teaching, having been commissioned by Baron Iwamura Michitoshi to cast bronze images of modern notables who took distinguished part in the grand work of the Restoration, and has already produced images of late Count Goto, of Marquis Ito and some others. Add. Ohara-machi, Koi, Tokyo. — p. 114 ([scan leaf 159](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n159/mode/1up))

**MOTONO, Ichiro** Hogaku-hakushi. Japanese Minister at St. Petersburg, b. Feb. '62, in Saga-ken; was translator of the Foreign Office '90, then Councillor '93, and transferred to the Secretaryship of Legation at St. Petersburg '96; he was appointed Minister Resident at Brussels '98; transferred to the Minister at Paris '01; finally to the present post early '05. He was a junior Delegate of Japan in the Peace Conference held at the Hague '99. Granted 2nd class Rising Sun. — p. 114 ([scan leaf 159](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n159/mode/1up))

**MOTO-ORI, Ho-ei** tutor of the Crown Prince in Japanese classics, b. '34 in Ise, is a grandson of the celebrated Moto-ori who flourished towards the end of the 18th century and was instrumental in reviving national literature and awakening the spirit of loyalty and patriotism to the Imp. Court. Add. Shin-ogawa-cho, U., T. — p. 114 ([scan leaf 159](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n159/mode/1up))

**MUDAGUCHI, Gengaku** President of the Tokyo Railway Co., b. '47 in Saga; became a civil official of the Department of Education and of the Department of Agriculture and Commerce, but resigned when Count Okuma (then Mr.) left the Government in '81. From that time till '93 he was engaged in various undertakings with the Count's backing, but generally ended in failure, but tide of fortune began to set in his favor with his appointment to President of the then Tokyo Horse Tram Co., which soon rose into prosperity under his management. With the conversion of horse trams into electric trams and the amalgamation of the three Tokyo Street electric trams, he was appointed President of the new concern. Add. 5, Shiba Park, Tokyo. — p. 114 ([scan leaf 159](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n159/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**MURAI, Kichibei** Pres. of Murai Bank, b. '64, second son of a merchant of Kaga, and adopted by his sister's husband who was running a small tobacco shop in Kyoto. The decision to manufacture cigarettes which had began to come in a large quantity from abroad was a turning point in his career. He found some means to go over to America, learned the art of cigarette making, and his first brand of cigarettes, first in Japan, was put on the market in '93. Extensively advertised his cigarettes soon spread all over Japan, and by '03, besides stopping the the import of foreign cigarettes, his brands and those of his imitators began to go to China and elsewhere in a large quantity. The America Tobacco Co. then offered to form a trust with him and the result was the Murai Brothers & Co. with a capital of ¥12,000,000 of which Murai's assets were valued at ¥5,000,000. This concern was purchased by the Government when it launched tobacco monopoly a year or two after. The Murais then started the Murai Bank, capital ¥1,000,000. They also undertake printing and cotton thread business. Add. Nagata-cho, Tokyo. — p. 115 ([scan leaf 160](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n160/mode/1up))

**MURATA, Tamotsu** Mem. House of Peers since '90, b. '45 in Osaka; a son of a retainer of Karatsu fief: was in the early part of this era an official of Justice Dep't, and was dispatched to England '70 to investigate criminal laws, of Education, Justice, etc.; has been devoting himself to promoting the cause of fishery enterprise since '90 when he left the Gov. service. Add. Takanawa, Tokyo. — p. 115 ([scan leaf 160](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n160/mode/1up))

**MURATA, Tanryo** b. July '72, is noted as a painter of historic subjects. Add. Nishi-machi, Shitaya, T. — p. 115 ([scan leaf 160](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n160/mode/1up))

**MURATA, Tsueyoshi** Maj.-Gen. (retired), Baron, inventor of the Murata rifle, Mem. House of Peers since '90. b. '38 in Satsuma; entered the Military early, and took part in the Civil War of '77, as a major. Add. Sambancho, Tokyo. — p. 115 ([scan leaf 160](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n160/mode/1up))

**MURAYAMA, Ryuhei** Proprietor of the Tokyo and Osaka Asahi, newspapers. b. at Wakayama '50; is a "Hamsworth" of Japan in a small way, and has by his shrewd business management raised the two Asahi to the present prosperity and importance. Once sat in the House for Osaka-fu. Add. Osaka Asahi Office, Osaka. — p. 115 ([scan leaf 160](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n160/mode/1up))

**MUTSU, Hirokichi** Count, 1st Sec. Embassy at London, b. '69, eldest son of the late Count Mutsu (d. '96) whose distinguished service in connection with the revision of treaty and conclusion of Shimonoseki treaty and will long remain on the page of Japanese dip. history. The new Count studied in England for several years beginning '87 and returned home as Barrister; passed exam. of dipl. service '95; 3rd Sec. of Legation (U.S.A.) '96; 2nd Sec. at Rome '00; transferred to present post '04. — p. 115 ([scan leaf 160](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n160/mode/1up))

**NABESHIMA, Marquis** (cr. '84. formerly Daimyo of Saga, Hizen). b. '49; is one of the few hereditary Peers who take active part in public service; studied in England for several years from '71; represented Japan at Rome '80-'82 and was for a long while the Master of Ceremonies at the Court; is Pres. Italian Association, Tokyo. Marchioness Nabeshima is the President of the Volunteer Nurse Association and is connected with various philanthropic works. Add. Nagata-cho, Tokyo. grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ.; long served as Public Procurator at Tokyo District Court and elsewhere, resigned '00; appointed Chief Councillor of the Treasury '01 but had to resign it owing to the abolition of the post. Attracted wide attention for his reclaimation undertaking in Koreafli is now conected with several concerns. Add. Shibuya near Tokyo. — p. 115 ([scan leaf 160](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n160/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**NABESHIMA, Keijiro** Sec. Residency-Gen. Seoul and Councillor of the Foreign Office, b. in Hizen; first entered the service of the Foreign Office '84; appointed attaché '86; was on suite of Prince Arisugawa when H.H. attended the late Queen Victoria's Diamond Jubilee '97; 1st Sec. Legation (Berlin) '99; transferred to London '02, then to U.S.A.; returned home about the beginning of the late War and had charge of the special correspondence affairs office created at that time. — p. 116 ([scan leaf 161](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n161/mode/1up))

**NAGAI, Kyuichiro** Yokohama Agent of N.Y.K.; long held posts in the Dep't of Education which he resigned about ten years ago and entered the N.Y.K. and was appointed its Shanghai Agent at first and then removed to the present post. Add. Yokohama Branch, N.Y.K. — p. 116 ([scan leaf 161](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n161/mode/1up))

**NAGAI, Nagayoshi** Rigaku-hakushi and Yakugaku-hakushi. Prof. Med. Col. Imp. Tokyo Univ.; b. '45 in Tokushima; was ordered to study his speciality in Germany ('70-'77) and again in France ('78-'83); appointed Prof. at the Imp. Tokyo Univ. and continues to hold the post. Mrs. Nagai is a German lady. Add. Minamicho, Aoyama, T. — p. 116 ([scan leaf 161](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n161/mode/1up))

**NAGAMORI, Tokichiro** b. in Saga, — p. 116 ([scan leaf 161](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n161/mode/1up))

**NAGANUMA, Shukei** metal-carver, Gold-medallist (Paris) and Prof. Gov. Fine Art Academy, b. '57 at Ichinoseki; studied the art under an Italian master then in the employ of the Government; proceeded to Italy '81 in company of the then Italian Minister and studied the art at Venice, returning home '87; revisited Italy ten years later in charge of the exhibition business of the Japan Fine Art Association. Add. Omote-cho, Koi., Tokyo. — p. 116 ([scan leaf 161](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n161/mode/1up))

**NAGAOKA, Gaishi** holder of 2nd class Golden Kite (war honor), Maj.-Gen., Com. 1st Brigade (inf.) b. '58 in Yamaguchi, 2nd son of S. Hori and adopted into the present family; sub.-Lieut. '79; entered the Mil. Staff Col. '83; rewarded with the 4th Order of Golden Kite in the Japan-China War in which he was on the staff of Gen. Nozu; ordered to proceed to Europe on official tour. '99; Maj.-Gen. '02; Vice-Chief of Staff during the late War till he was succeeded by Lieut.-Gen. Fukushima, in Mar., '06. Add. Harajiku, Aoyama, T. — p. 116 ([scan leaf 161](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n161/mode/1up))

**NAGASAKI, Shogo** Councillor of the Imperial Court and Dir. Purveyors' Bureau, Personal Secretary to the Minister of Imp. Household, b. Kagoshima '55; studied in America and England; returned home about '80 and has been connected with the Court ever since till rising to the present high position. Has almost invariably been appointed a member of the Reception Committee whenever a distinguished guest visited Japan, and has frequently been sent abroad on the suite of one Prince or another. Decorated with K. C. M. G. by King Edward on the occasion of the Garter Mission. Add. Ichiban-cho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 116 ([scan leaf 161](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n161/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**NAGAYO, Shokichi** Dr. Igaku-hakushi, and prop. of the Nagayo Stomach and Intestine Disease Hosp., b. '66 in Tokyo, eldest son of the late Dr. Sensai Nagayo, one of the pioneer doctors of western school; studied med. first at the Imp. Tokyo Univ. and then at Berlin and founded the present hospital about 10 years ago. Add. Uchisaiwai-cho, Koj., T. — p. 117 ([scan leaf 162](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n162/mode/1up))

**NAITO, Hisahiro** pioneer of the kerosene industry in Echigo and Pres. of the Nippon Kerosene Co., b. Ishiji, Echigo '59, of a wealthy family; took active part in local politics and returned frequently to the House of Representatives; founded the Kerosene Company '88 which has proved an immense success. — p. 117 ([scan leaf 162](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n162/mode/1up))

**NAKABASHI, Tokugoro** President of the Osaka Merchant Ship Co., and Ujigawa Electric Co., b. at Kanazawa '60, grad. the Imp. Tokyo Univ. '84; was long an official in the Dep't of Com. where he occupied the post of Director of the Accountant Bureau, which resigned about '96 to take charge of the present Co. which he revived to the present state of prosperity. Add. Osaka Shosen Kaisha. — p. 117 ([scan leaf 162](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n162/mode/1up))

**NAKAHAMA, To-ichiro** Dr., Igaku-hakushi, b. in Tokyo, '57; is a son of the celebrated fisherman Manjiro who was drifted to America and came back on Perry's ships; is a specialist of internal diseases. Add. Shimo-Nibancho, Tokyo. — p. 117 ([scan leaf 162](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n162/mode/1up))

**NAKAKOJI, Ren** Vice-Minister of Communications, b. '66 in Tokushima-ken; studied law at a private school and passed the Judge and Pub. Procurator Examination; sat long on the Bench either as Judge or Public Procurator in Tokyo and elsewhere and won a high reputation by judging the assassination case of Kim Okun and the Tokyo Water-pipe scandal; appointed Chief Councillor for the Dep't of Com. '01; resigned in a year or two in connection with the emolument question of the Bench and then filled the post of Director of the Seoul-Fusan R'ly '03-'04; Dir. of the Police Bureau, Home Office '4-6; to be promoted to the present post on the formation of the Saionji Ministry. Add. Hikawa, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 117 ([scan leaf 162](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n162/mode/1up))

**NAKAMUDA, Kuranosuke** Vice-Admiral (retired), Viscount, (cr. '84), Privy Councillor, b. '31 in Saga; is one of the pioneer naval officers, and did much in laying the foundation of the future of fleet of Japan. Add. Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 117 ([scan leaf 162](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n162/mode/1up))

**NAKAMURA, Kaku** holder of 2nd class Golden Kite and of Grand Cordon of Rising Sun (war honor), Lieut.-Gen. and Com. of the Division on duty in Korea; b. '54 in Shiga; Sub-Lieut. '75; took part in the Japan-China War as Reg. Commander and was rewarded with 4th Order of Golden Kite; Maj-Gen. '99: is widely known as commander of the forlorn-hope that attacked positions east of the R'ly pass at Port Arthur on Nov. 26, when he was slightly wounded: was promoted Lieut.-Gen. early '06 and appointed Chief of Staff of the Military Education Board, and then transferred to the present post early this year. — p. 117 ([scan leaf 162](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n162/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**NAKANO, Buyei** President of the Tokyo Chamber of Commerce b. '49 at Takamatsu; before '81 was a civil official, but resigning the post he entered business. Was for a long the Director of the Tokyo Tramway Co. (forerunner of the present Tokyo Electric Car Co.), and has been elected Pres. of the Tokyo Stock Exchange a year or two ago. He sat in the House from '90 to 1902 as a Progressive M.P. Add. Moto-machi, Hongo, T. — p. 118 ([scan leaf 163](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n163/mode/1up))

**NAKASHIMA, Rikizo** Professor of Philosophy, Imp. Tokyo Univ., b. '58 in Tamba; studied in U.S.A., Britain and Germany '77 to '90; was given present chair '92. Add. Wakamiya-cho, U., Tokyo. — p. 118 ([scan leaf 163](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n163/mode/1up))

**NAMBU, Heinai** Maj.-Gen. and Dir. Mil. Staff Col. b. '56 in Ishikawa; Sub.-Lieut. '77; Col. '99; Maj.-Gen. shortly before the late War in which as Com. of a Brigade of the 3rd Div. (Nagoya) he won universal renown for having resisted the enemy's one crops at Likanpao on Mar. 8' in the great battle of Mukden. Was granted 2nd class Golden Kite and of Rising Sun (war honor). Add. Mil. Staff Col., Tokyo. — p. 118 ([scan leaf 163](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n163/mode/1up))

**NAMBU, Kamco** Baron (cr. '96), Privy Councillor, ex-Pres. of the Supreme Court, b. '45 in Tosa, 2nd son of a retainer of that clan: took some active part in the work of Restoration; was appointed a junior Judge '72 and subsequently rose steadily in the legal service till he occupied post of chief Judge at the Tokyo Appeal Court '91 and promoted to the supreme post '96. Add. Azabu, Honmura-cho, T. — p. 118 ([scan leaf 163](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n163/mode/1up))

**NAMBU, Kyugo** Kogaku-hakushi, Dir. Mining Dep't, Mitsubishi, b. '55 in Fukui; studied at the Imp. Univ. '69-'74; was among the first batch of students sent abroad by the Gov. for prosecution of study and studied mining in U.S.A. '75-'80, to be engaged the following year by the Mitsubishi to take charge of Takashima coal. Has ever since connected with this firm. Add. Benten-cho, Ushigome, T. — p. 118 ([scan leaf 163](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n163/mode/1up))

**NAMIKAWA, Sosuke** a celebrated cloisonne artist and porcelain painter, b. '47 at Nagoya; has done more than any person in carrying cloisonne work to the present state of perfection. He took charge of the work of decorating a hall in the Imp. Court, and is an Art Commissioner to it. Add. Shim-emon-cho, N., T. — p. 118 ([scan leaf 163](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n163/mode/1up))

**NAMURA, Taizo** Mem. House of Peers since '94, b. at Nagasaki, '41; sat at the Bench for many years ultimately rising to the top of the service as Procurator-Gen. of the Supreme Court '86 and chief of the same '92-'93, which resigned '94 and has devoted himself to business as Pre. of Tokyo Type Foundry. Add. Fujimi-cho, Koj., T. — p. 118 ([scan leaf 163](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n163/mode/1up))

**NANJO, Bunyu** Bungaku-hakushi. b. May '49 in Mino; grad. Oxford Univ. '84 where he studied Sanskrit under Max. Muller. Was a Professor of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. Add. Shimo-rokuban-cho, T. — p. 118 ([scan leaf 163](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n163/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**NARABARA, Shigeru** Baron (cr. '96), Governor of Okinawa, b. '34 in Satsuma; was on the suite of the Prince Shimazu when the Namamugi tragedy took place '62 that led to the bombardment of Kagoshima, by British warship; was once President of the Nippon R'ly Co., and Gov. of Shizuoka '83; occupies the present post from '92. — p. 119 ([scan leaf 164](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n164/mode/1up))

**NARUSE, Ninzo** President of the Women's University, b. '60 in Yoshiki, Yamaguchi; was primary school teacher before he started the Baikwa Girls' School at Osaka '78; started a similar school Niigata '86-'90 and with its close proceeded to America where he investigated female education, returning home '96. He then actively canvassed among wealthy classes, representing necessity of establishing a higher female institution. The movement bore fruit and the present school was opened in April '01. Add. Women's Univ., Mejiro. — p. 119 ([scan leaf 164](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n164/mode/1up))

**NATSUME, Kinnosukê** pen-name Sōsèki, is a noted author who has just joined the staff of the Tokyo Asahi by resigning the post of Prof. of the College Lit., Imp. Tokyo Univ.; was born '66 at Matsuyama, Iyo, and graduated course of English Literature at Imp. Tokyo Univ. in '90. Teacher of English at Kumamoto High School for several years after graduation, was dispatched to England to further prosecute study '98-'01. On returning home he was given chair at his alma mater. Has written "I am a Cat" and other humorous works. Add. Nishikata, Hongo, T. — p. 119 ([scan leaf 164](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n164/mode/1up))

**NISHI, Kwanjiro** General, Baron, (cr. '95), b. '46 in Satsuma; entered Army while young, promoted Major-General in '89; commanded a Brigade of the 1st Army Division (Tokyo) during the Japan-China War and fought at Port Arthur, &c. under Marshal Oyama; was created a Baron and decorated with the 3rd Order of Golden Kite. In the late War he commanded the 2nd Army Division (Sendai) and took part in Manchurian campaigns as far as the battle of Shaho; then promoted full General June '04 and appointed Commander of the Garrison of Liaotung and removed May '05 to the Inspector-Gen. of Education. He started for London in Mar. this year as a member of Prince Fushimi's suite, on mission to the Court of St. James. Holds 1st class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun and Paulonia (war honor). Add. Shimo-Nibancho, Koj., T. — p. 119 ([scan leaf 164](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n164/mode/1up))

**NISHI, Tokujiro** Baron, (cr. '96), a Privy Councillor, b. in Satsuma '47; entered diplomatic career early; Minister at St. Petersburg '86-'96; was the Minister of Foreign Affairs Nov. '97-June '98 (under the Matsukata and Ito Ministries); Japanese Minister at Peking on the occasion of the North-China trouble and was one of those who sustained the memorable siege. Add. Kogai-cho, Azabu, T. — p. 119 ([scan leaf 164](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n164/mode/1up))

**NISHIJIMA, Sukeyoshi** holder of 2nd class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun (war honor), Lieut.-Gen. and Com. 2nd (Sendai) Div., b. '48 in Yamaguchi and was adopted by a relative also of the same name: Sub.-Lieut. '73; granted 4th Class of Golden Kite for his service in the Japan-China War: Maj.-Gen. '98; Com. a Brigade of the Div. in the late and was raised to take its command on the transfer of Baron Gen. Nishi to other post. landscape painting. Add. Uchi-saiwai-cho, Tokyo. — p. 119 ([scan leaf 164](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n164/mode/1up))

**NITOBE, Inazo** Nogaku-hakushi, Dif. First Tokyo High School, was b. '63 and grad. Sapporo Agri. Col. '81; studied agr'al economy at the John's Hopkin's Univ., Baltimore, U.S.A., and at Bonn; Prof. of the Sapporo Col. for severaal years after his return; then official of Formosa which left '04 to fill chair of Economy of the Univ. at Kyoto. His little work in English, "Bushido," published at Philadelphia in '98 has been translated into several languages and is now a classic about Japan and Japanese; also wrote "Nogyo Honron" (New Agriculture) and others. Mrs. Nitobe is an American lady. Add. 1st High School, Tokyo. — p. 120 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n165/mode/1up))

**NODZU, Michitsura** Marshal. Count (cr. '95). Mem. Supreme Military Council. b. '40 in Satsuma; fought as a Colonel at the Civil War of '77; Lieut.-Gen. in '85; General in '94: defeated the Chinese in the memorable battle of Pingyang at the head of the Hiroshima Division, and on Marshal Yamagata's being recalled home by the Emperor in consideration of his weak health the Gen. was ordered to succeed him and take command of the First Army; Inspect-Gen. of Education before he was appointed Commander of the Fourth Army in the late War; appointed to the present post Dec. '05. Was graduated 1st class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun and Paulonia for his service in the War. Add. Shin-machi. Akasaka, T. — p. 120 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n165/mode/1up))

**NOGUCHI, Sho-hin** a lady painter, b. '47 at Osaka: is a master of — p. 120 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n165/mode/1up))

**NOGI, Kiten** General and Mem. Supreme Military Council (app. Dec. '05.) Pres. Noble School, Baron, (cr. 1895), b. '49 in Choshu, is one of the most soldiery soldiers, perhaps too strict and Spartan for this effeminate and finical age; Captain in the Civil War of '77; as Commander of a brigade fought at Kinchow and Port Arthur, in the Japan-China War; rewarded with Peerage and 3rd class of Golden Kite; promoted to Lieut.-Gen. with Command of the 2nd Division Sendai). '95; for a short while the Governor-Gen. of Formosa '96, but was soon returned to his congenial life as Commander of the 11th Division (Marugame); resigned the post about the fall of 1900 and, like a Roman General, took to farming, till, on the outbreak of the late War, he was ordered to attack Port Arthur as Commander of the 3rd Army, he having been promoted to full General in June '04; the part he has undertaken in the second capture of Port Arthur and the turning movement in the Battle of Mukden is still fresh in the memory of all the world. The loss of his two sons, both young officers in a Division composing his Army, one at Nansan and the other at Port Arthur has won profound sympathy both at home and abroad. Was invested with 1st class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun and Paulonia. Add. Shin-machi, Akasaka. T. — p. 120 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n165/mode/1up))

**NOMURA, Bunkyo** Japanese painter, b. '54 in Kyoto and learned painting of the Southern Chinese school; teacher at the Peers' School '89-96; painted 15 kinds of animals to the order of the Imperial Court '98 and they are now kept at the Imp. Armory. Add. Motozono-cho, Koj., T. — p. 120 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n165/mode/1up))

**NOMURA, Yasushi** Viscount, (cr. 1887), a Privy Councillor, b. '42 in Choshu, and played active part in the grand work of the Restoration, and was given a high post on the formation of the rehabilitated Imperial Government; Minister of Home-Affairs in Ito Ministry '94-'96; Minister of Communication in the Matsukata Ministry '96-'97; appointed to the present post in 1900. Add. Azabu Mikawadai, Tokyo. — p. 121 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n166/mode/1up))

**NOZAKI, Keizo** Solit.-Gen. of the Supreme Court, b. '52 in Hiroshima; first entered the service of Justice Dep't 75, public Procurator 82; despatched abroad '89 to investigate judical system; appointed Chief Public Procurator Yokohama District Court the following year; subsequently filled a similar post at various District and Appeal Courts till he was appointed to the present post '98. Add. Ichibei-cho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 121 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n166/mode/1up))

**O-AKI, Kikusaburo** a millionaire shipbuilder, b. '45 at Toda, Izu, son of an obscure ship-carpenter and one of the pioneer ship-carpenters of foreign style vessels; started in Tokyo '79 a small shipyard; leased '83 No. 4 old fort at Shinagawa and this miniature shipbuilding works built on a little ground standing in the sea has grown to the present flourishing condition solely by his indefatigable diligence and integrity, now owing about 20 small steamers run along the shores of Tokyo Bay. He is building a large shipyard at Toba, Shima, the present yard proving too small to meet the growing requirements. Add. Kita Shinagawa, Tokyo. — p. 121 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n166/mode/1up))

**OGATA, Gekko** b. '55, is perhaps the most celebrated living painter of the Ukiyoe school, which is chiefly devoted in depicting manners and customs in contrast to other schools whose ambition lies in choosing more "philosophical" subjects. Add. Oke-cho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 121 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n166/mode/1up))

**OGATA, Masanori** Dr., Igaku-hakushi, b. '53 in Kumamoto-ken; studied medicine in Germany '81-'84; is a follower of the famous pathologist Virchow, and frequent has been the controversy between him and Dr. Kitazato on points of medical theory as was the case between their respective masters in Germany; is Prof. at the Col. of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. Add. Higashikata-machi, Hg., T. — p. 121 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n166/mode/1up))

**OGAWA, Mataji** Gen., Baron (cr. '95), b. '48 at Kokura; a Major of the Kumamoto garrison when besieged by the rebels in the Civil War '77; chief of staff to the First Army at the time of the Japan-China War under Marshal Yamagata; created a Peer and prom. Lieut.-Gen. for the service rendered during the war, with command of the 4th Division (Osaka), and the grant of 3rd class Golden Kite. In the late War he led his Division in Gen. Oku's Second Army, till wounded in the Battle of Liaoyang he was sent home; promoted to full Gen. Jan. '05. Holds 2nd class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun. Add. Osaka. — p. 121 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n166/mode/1up))

**OGAWA, Zenkichi** Dir. Nippon Yusen Kaisha, b. '55 in Hyogo-ken; studied in U.S.A. and on returning home '75 was appointed teacher first at the Gov. Nagoya English School then at that in Osaka, which latter he resigned about '77 to enter the Mitsubishi Co. On its amalgamation with the Kyodo Unyu to form the present N.Y.K. he followed the new Co. where he holds a post of Director. Add. Goken-cho, U., T. on the suspicions of dynamite affairs, and acquitted after several month's confinement; travelled abroad; appointed by the Ito Ministry Resident to Korea '92-'93; left the Gov. service and once more resumed his place in the Opposition under the lead of Count Okuma; twice re-entered the Gov., first as Vice-Minister of Agr. and Com. when the Matsukata Cabinet concluded an entente with the Progressives '96-'97, and next as Minister of the same Dep't '98 in the Okuma-Itagaki coalition ministry. He has sat in the House since '98. Diplomacy is what he considers as his forte. Add. Minamitenma-cho, Y., T. — p. 121 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n166/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**OHASHI, Shintaro** Prop. of the Hakubunkan, publishing house, Vice-Pres. Tokyo Chamber of Commerce, etc; b. '63, eldest son of the late Sahei Ohashi, founder of the house; studied at the Dōnin-Sha and was his father's right-hand man in carrying the publishing business to the prosperity it attained. In pursuance of his father's will be founded the Ohashi Library a few years ago at a big outlay. Sat for a while in the House of Representatives for Tokyo. Add. Kami-Rokuban-cho, Tokyo. — p. 122 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n167/mode/1up))

**OHASHI, Suiseki** Gold-medallist at Paris ('00) and St. Louis ('03), b. '65 in Ogaki; first studied painting under the late Watanabe Shokwa and at first followed the Chinese style of southern school; afterward turned his power to depicting animals, especially tigers for which he has become famous both at home and abroad, his productions having won high-grade medals at domestic and international exhibitions. Add. Ogaki, Mino. — p. 122 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n167/mode/1up))

**OISHI, Masami** M.P., leader of the Progressive Party, was b. '55 in Tosa, and, like most prominent men born in that province took active part in taking up the cause of democracy. Severed connection with Count Itagaki's Liberal Party '82; arrested '85 with the late Baba who died prematurely in U.S.A. — p. 122 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n167/mode/1up))

**OKA, Genkei** Dr., Igaku-hakushi, chief physician-in-waiting to His Majesty; is one of the pioneer grad. of the Med. Col. of what has subsequently become the Imp. Tokyo Univ. For some years he further pursued the study of his special line in Germany. Add. Kudanshita, Tokyo. — p. 122 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n167/mode/1up))

**OKABE** Viscount (cr. '84), formerly Daimyo of Kishiwada, Izumi, Mem. House of Peers, b. '54; studied in America and England '75-'83; appointed Councillor of the Japanese Legation in London '86; Vice-Minister for Foreign Affairs '89-'90; was appointed Minister Plenip. unattached '91; Governor of Tokyo-fu '94-'96; he has leased a wide tract of land in northern Korea, with the object of setting an example to his fellow Peers, to convert it into a model plantation. Add. Waseda-Takatadai, Tokyo. — p. 122 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n167/mode/1up))

**OKAMURA, Teruhiko** Hogaku-hakushi, lawyer, b. '55 at Osaka; studied law at London '76-'81; afterward became a judge and was appointed President of the Yokohama Local Court, but retired in '91, since which he has been practising law; was dispatched to London several years ago by the Government in connection with the Chishima-Ravenna collision case, which was decided by the Privy Council in favor of Japan. Add. San-ban-cho, Koj., Tokyo. the occasion of the memorable split and was one of those who founded the Nippon Fine Art Institute; travelled though Europe, 1900. Won a Gold Medal at Paris Fair '1900 and Grand Prize at St. Louis. . Add. Yanaka, Uyeno, Tokyo. — p. 122 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n167/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**OKAUCHI, Shigetoshi, Baron (cr. 1900)** Mem. House of Peers and Lord in Waiting of Kinkei Hall, b. in Tosa '42; was a staunch Imperialist in the troubled time preceding the Restoration; appointed Judge '69; sent abroad to Europe '7'; Chief Public Procurator and Chief Judge of an Appeal Court '73; transferred to the Court of Cassation and, then to the Senate. He was raised to a Peer in consideration of his service at the time of the Restoration and is one of the leading members of the Seiyukai. Add. Yayoi-cho, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 123 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n168/mode/1up))

**OKAZAKI, Kunisuke** Dir. of the Furukawa Min. Co., but is more widely known as politician of acute discernment and fertile brain, though keeping aloof from it some years. He was a confidante of the late Count Mutsu and was 1st Councillor of the Communication Dep't when the late Hoshi held the chair. Add. Akashi-cho, Kyo., T. — p. 123 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n168/mode/1up))

**OKAZAKI, Sessei** veteran metalcaster and modeller, b. in Fushimi, and studied the art under his father and others; Prof. of Gov. Fine Art Academy '90; was on Japanese Commission at the time of Chicago World's Fair, and finished the casting of Nanko's bronze image in front of the Imperial Palace '94; resigned the Professorship '98 on — p. 123 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n168/mode/1up))

**OKANO, Keijiro** Dir. Legislative Bureau and Prof. of Law Col., Imp. Tokyo Univ., b. in Tokyo '65; grad. Law in said university '86 and further studied mercantile Law in the Univ. Hall, after which he filled chair assist. Prof. at the alma mater. Studied in Germany '91-'95 and took chair of Mercantile Law on returning home; appointed Councillor of Dep't Agr. and Com. as additional duty '98 and had charge of Mercantile Law in the Code Revision Commission's work. Appointed to the present post on the formation of the present Ministry. He was granted 2nd class Rising Sun '07 for his service in connection with the Imperial Family Affairs Investigation Commission. Add. Tenjin, Yushima, Tokyo. — p. 123 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n168/mode/1up))

**OKAKURA, Kakuzo** art critic and author, b. '62 in Fukui; grad. Politics at Tokyo Univ. '80, and was appointed an official of the Dep't of Edu. in charge of music affairs. Travelled abroad on official business '86-'87 and was appointed manager of the then established Fine Art Academy, to be raised in time to post of Director, but which post he had to resign '98 owing to some collision with the authorities. Started with Hashimoto Gaho and several others who resigned at the same time a private institute, the Nippon Fine Art Institute, which at one time almost threw the Gov. institute into shade, but last year from financial difficulty, and after the suspension of some years, it was removed to a village in Ibaragi-ken. He was engaged by the Boston Museum in '06 to arrange Japanese art objects in its collection; has written "The Intellectual Awakening of Japan" and "Japanese Tea Ceremonial," "The Ideals of the East," all in English. Add. Yanaka, Ueno Park. — p. 123 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n168/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**OKAZAWA, Sei** Baron, (cr. '95), Gen. and Chief Aide-de-Camp to His Majesty, eldest son of a retainer of the clan of Choshu, b. '44; took some active part in the work of Restoration; Sub.-Lieut. in '71, Lieut.-Gen. '95, and full Gen. '05; was appointed to the present post '94 prior to which he held post of Brigade Commander, Chief Staff officer to the Military Inspection Board and Vice-Min. of War. Granted K.C.V.O. by King Edward, and was deco'ted 2nd class Golden Kite in connection with the late War. Add. Shinsaka-machi, Ak., T. — p. 124 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n169/mode/1up))

**OKOSHI, Seitoku** ex-Min. Resident to Brazil, b. in Tokyo; first entered the Foreign Office '63; appointed Consul at Lyon '85-'93; Consul-Gen. at Shanghai '93-94; transferred to be Chief of Yokohama Customs '94; Bureau Dr. at the Communication Dep't '98; Min. Resident and Con.-Gen. at Brazil '99 which he resigned '04 to become a partner of the Anglo-Japanese Syndicate. — p. 124 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n169/mode/1up))

**OKU** General, Chief of General Staff and Mem. Supreme Military Council., Baron, (cr. '95), b. '44 Fukuoka-ken; entered the Army '71; won distinction in the Civil War of '77 for having cut through the besieging lines of the rebels round Kumamoto and thus establishing connection with the reinforcements that were coming to its rescue. Commanded the 5th Division (Hiroshima) in the Japan-China War and was rewarded with Peerage and the 3rd class of the Golden Kite; Commander of the Tokyo Bay Defence, Commander of the Eastern Military Districts, and other high posts, meanwhile promoted to full Gen. '03; attended the Durbar at Delhi, led Second Army in the late War and for his distinguished service he was granted 1st class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun and Paulonia. He was appointed to the present post when the late Vis. Kodama suddenly died in Nov. '06. Add. Suidō-cho, Koi., Tokyo. — p. 124 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n169/mode/1up))

**OKUBO, Shunya** Lieut.-Gen. and Commander of the 3rd (Nagoya) Div., b. in Shizuoka-ken '46; was sent to France to study military science as early as '70; Maj. '80, Col. '89, Dir. Toyama School '90 and that of the Cadet School '91; chief staff officer to 2nd Div. '92; Maj.-Gen. '94; Lieut.-Gen. '00 and chief staff officer of Mil. Education Board; transferred to the command of the Div. '02, and fought in Marshal Nozu's Fourth Army in the late War, for which he received 2nd class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun. — p. 124 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n169/mode/1up))

**OKUDA, Yoshito** M.P., Hogakuhakushi, b. '60 in Tottori-ken; grad. Law Course of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. '84, and was at once appointed an official of the Dep't of Agr. and Com. and Director of Patent Bureau '90; transferred to Director of the Official Gazette Bureau; Chief Secretary of the House of Representatives '96; Vice-Minister of Agr. and Com. '98, and that of Edu. '99; Director of the Legislative Bureau '00-02, and a member of the Imperial Family System Investigation Commission with Marq. Ito as chief, and on its dissolution '07 he was appointed Commissioner to be Court. He was granted 2nd class Rising Sun for his service. He has sat in the House since '03, and is a founder of the Hogaku-in (private Law Col.) and travelled abroad '99. Add. Motozono-cho, Koj., T. — p. 124 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n169/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**OKUMA, Shige-nobu** Count, (cr. '87), was b. Feb. '38 in Saga, and shares with Marquis Ito the honor of being two of the ablest living statesmen of Japan. In prestige, mental vigor and intellectual activity the two really stand on the same plane, and both are alike in entertaining progressive views. There is really no political rivalry to separate the two though this compatibility can hardly exist between the Count and Marquis Yamagata who is a statesman of conservative ideas. Fate, however, has denied to the subject of this sketch as it has allowed to the two Marquises as better opportunities of directing the affairs of state, for he does not belong to either of the two ruling clans, Choshu or Satsuma. For about ten years, i.e., '73-81 he had charge of the Treasury first as Vice-Minister and then as full Minister, and this post he had to resign and retire from Government service ostensibly because his memorial urging the Government to introduce representative government was rejected by his colleagues, but probably from some deeper causes which history will show. An interesting episode in his official career was the military mission he undertook of suppressing the insurrection of Eto in '74. The immediate result of his resignation was the formation of the Kaishin-to, the forerunner of the present Progressive Party. In '88, the year preceding the promulgation of the Constitution, he filled the chair of the Foreign Office in the Cabinet of his great friend, the late Count Kuroda, and undertook the important task of revising the Treaties concluded with the Powers before the Restoration. The clause in the draft treaty for appointing foreign judges in the Supreme Court, had evoked the discontent of a section of public, and Kurushima Tsuneki hurled a bomb at his coach. It injured his leg which was amputed. When the Kaishin-to joined hands with the 2nd Matsukata Ministry '96, the Count re-entered the Cabinet as Foreign Minister and Minister of Agriculture and Commerce. This alliance soon broke down as was the formation in June '98 of the Okuma-Itagaki Coalition Ministry in which the Count assumed the Premiership with additional duty as Minister of Foreign Affairs. In Feb. '07, at general meeting of the Progressive M.P.s he declared his resignation of the leadership. Thus ends, so far, the Count's official career, which even his admirers can hardly call it a success. But he is a many-sided man and can boast success in other spheres, especially in the founding of Waseda Senmon Gakko (now Waseda Univ.) in '82 and for which he has just been installed as Chancellor; while horticulture is his hobby and his collection of tropical plants, chiefly orchilds, and other rare plants is perhaps the best in Japan. Unlike Marquis Ito the Count has never been abroad, and for his son and heir he adopted second son of Count Matsura, as husband to his daughter. Add. Waseda, Tokyo. pany with Mrs. Okura. He holds a court title and wears the 3rd Order of the Rising Sun, and his private collection of art objects is famous. Add. Aoi-zaka, Tokyo. — p. 125 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n170/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**OKURA, Heizo** Lieut.-Gen. (Cav.), b. '52 in Okayama; second son of I. Ono and adopted into the present family; Sub.-Lieut. (Cav.) '75; Col. '95; Maj.-Gen. '98; to be promoted to the present rank quite recently. He is one of the highest authorities on horses in Japan and has taken charge of the business of rising and improving horses for military service. Holds 3rd class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun (war honor. Add. Nakazato, U., Tokyo. — p. 126 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n171/mode/1up))

**OKURA, Kihachiro** one of the new-made millionaires, b. '37 in Echigo. Removed to Tokyo early in the era and started business of selling arms and ammunition, from which he derived a big profit; opened a branch store in London '74 and ever since he has kept up an export and import business, especially the latter in the shape of machineries, electric plant, and such big things. In '99 he founded a private business school to commemorate the 30th anniversary of his business enterprise, by endowing it with the fund of half a million yen. He travelled twice through Europe and America first in '72 and second '00 to visit the World Fair at Paris in company with Mrs. Okura. He holds a court title and wears the 3rd Order of the Rising Sun, and his private collection of art objects is famous. Add. Aoi-zaka, Tokyo. — p. 126 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n171/mode/1up))

**OMIWA, Chobei** b. in Osaka about '43. Has had an eventful career both as business man and politician; was arrested '78 on the charge of complicity with the Satsuma rebels of '77 but was acquitted on ground of insufficient evidence; took prominent part in the democratic movement prior to the advent of Constitutional regime; was chairman of the Osaka Municipal Council; returned M.P. from Osaka. It was chiefly as mint-master of the Korean Gov. '91-'95 that his name was chiefly known to general public. Add. Osaka. — p. 126 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n171/mode/1up))

**OMORI, Fusakichi** Seismologist and Prof. of Imp. Tokyo Univ., b. '68 in Fukui; graduated the Col. of Science of the Univ. '87, first studying physics but subsequently turning to seismology; further prosecuted study in Italy and Germany '94-'97, to be appointed Prof. at the alma mater on returning home; attended the Seismological conference held in Germany '01 and '03; sent to India '04 and to San Francisco '06 to investigate the disastrous earthquakes that visited the two places in the respective years. Has invented various seismological instruments, and has also contributed valuable work to the world's literature of this science. Add. Suido-machi, Koi., Tokyo. — p. 126 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n171/mode/1up))

**OMORI, Sho-ichi** Gov. of Kyoto, b. '56 at Fuchu, Shizuoka-ken. He began his career in civil service as a clerk in the Mint '73, to be transferred shortly to the Dep't of Justice, then to the Home Office with which he has since been connected, was Dir. Local Adm. Bureau '9; Gov. of Nagasaki '96; then of Hyogo, and after a short service as Vice-Min. of Home Office he was transferred to the present post. — p. 126 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n171/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**ONO, Kozan** b. '13 at Hikone and enjoyed a wide celebrity till a few years ago for his Chinese verses and caligraphy; was granted a court title '00 in consideration of his long service in the cause of culture and refinement. Add. Sugamo, Tokyo. — p. 127 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n172/mode/1up))

**ONO, Kiwokei** b. '45 in Shinano; is one of the leading merchants of Yokohama where he has been engaged in silk trade since it was opened to foreign trade. Is a promoter of the Yokohama Specie Bank and is also connected with several important undertakings carried on in the port. — p. 127 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n172/mode/1up))

**O-OKA, Ikuzo** M.P., a leader of the Seiyukai, b. June '56 in Choshu; was long a lawyer in Tokyo which business he has subsequently deserted to devote himself to politics in which he was once a member of the Nation Unionist Association and now one of the Seiyukai. Is the chairman of the City Council, Tokyo, and owns the Chu-o. Add. Yamashiro-cho, Tokyo. — p. 127 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n172/mode/1up))

**OSAWA, Kenji** Dr., Igaku-hakushi Mem. House of Peers, b. '52 in Mikawa; pursued medical study in Germany '70-'74 and '78-'82; since then Professor of the Medical Course of the Imp. Tokyo Univ. Add. Higashi-kata-machi, Hg., Tokyo. — p. 127 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n172/mode/1up))

**OSAKO, Shobin** Baron, Gen. and ex-Commander of the 7th (Hokkaido) Div., b. in Kagoshima '44; Capt. '72; Major '78; was created a Baron with a further reward of 3rd class Golden Kite for his service in the Japan-China War; Vice-Chief of General Staff Board '98-'00 to transferred to the above post. His Division formed part of Gen. Nogi's Army and took part in the arduous operations at Port Arthur and in the memorable turning movement in the battle of Mukden. Promoted full General May '06, and granted 2nd class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun. — p. 127 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n172/mode/1up))

**OSHIMA, Hisanao** Baron, Gen. Com. of the 9th (Kanazawa) Div., b. Akita '48; Lieut. '71; Major '74 and took part in the Satsuma Civil War, Lieut.-Col. '81; Maj.-Gen. '92. He was at the head of a Brigade in the Japan-China War and was rewarded with Barony and 3rd class of the Golden Kite. Promoted to Lieut.-Gen. '98 and appointed to the present post. His Division formed part of Gen. Nogi's first investing Army at Port Arthur and next the outflanking corps at Mukden. Promoted full General May, '06, and granted 2nd class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun. — p. 127 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n172/mode/1up))

**OSHIMA, Michitaro** Chief Eng. of the Gov. Steel Works at Wakamatsu, b. '61 at Morioka; entered the then Gov. Univ. '70 first in the Course of Med. but afterward removed to the Course of Science; proceeded to Germany and graduated Metallurgy at Freiberg '79-'81; appointed Eng. to the Imperial Estate '90, Chief Eng. of the Steel Works since '96. Add Steel Works, Wakamatsu. — p. 127 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n172/mode/1up))

**OSHIMA, Yoshimasa** Baron, Gen. and Gov.-Gen. Kwantung District (Liaotung), b. '50 in Choshu; Sub.-Lieut. '75; was Com. of the Mixed Brigade that was sent to Korea in '94 on the outbreak of the Tonghak trouble and had the honor of inflicting a first blow on the Chinese troops at Songhwan. His frontal attack in the battle of Pingyang, intended as a feint, cost rather heavy casualties; was rewarded with peerage and 3rd class of the Golden Kite. During the late War he was in Gen. Oku's Army at the head of the 3rd Div. and fought as far as the Battle of Mukden in which he was in the Central (Nozu's) Army; then transferred to the present post, and granted 2nd class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon of Rising Sun. Add. Port Arthur. has raised himself to the present influential position from obscurity, having been for long a clerk at a foreign firm in Yokohama dealing in tea; set up a tea store at his own account number of years ago and is now the foremost tea exporter. Has taken great pains in maintaining the fame of Japanese tea in America in the face of aggressive measures taken by its Ceylon rival. Is the chairman of the Japan Tea Guild and President of the 74th Bank, Yokohama. Add. Moto-hama-cho, Yokohama. — p. 127 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n172/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**OTA, Minêsaburo** Chief Secretary House of Peers, b. '61 in Fukuoka; finished Law, Imperial Miv. about '85. Add. House of Peers, Tokyo. — p. 128 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n173/mode/1up))

**OTAGIRI, Masunosuké** Director of the Yokohama Specie Bank and Chief of its Branch in Shanghai, was b. '65; was sent by the Foreign Office to Tientsin in '86 to study Chinese, and subsequently till '06 when he entered the Bank, he was connected almost without interruption with consular service in China, ultimately rising to General-Consulship at Shanghai. — p. 128 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n173/mode/1up))

**OTAGURO, Jugoro** who has charge of the Mitsui Shiba-ura Eng. Works, b. in Tokyo '67; grad. High Com. School '90 to be appointed for a while Man. Osaka Com. Museum; entered the Mitsui's service '94. Add. Shiba-ura Eng. Works, T. — p. 128 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n173/mode/1up))

**OTANI, Kahei** b. Dec. '44 in Ise; — p. 128 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n173/mode/1up))

**OTANI, Kodzui** Count (suc. '03), b. at Kyoto, Dec. '76; is the Lord Abbot of the Western Hongwan Temple, one of the two greatest Buddhist centres in Japan. Stayed long in Europe for prosecution of study, and started home '02 via the Central Asia, but was obliged to hurry home before he could carry out his programme on learning the death of his father. He married Princess Kadzuko (elder sister of the Crown Princess), 2nd daughter of the late Prince Kujo, Jan. '98, and with the Countess visited Saghalien and China on religious mission. Add. Nishi-Hongwan-ji Kyoto. — p. 128 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n173/mode/1up))

**OTANI, Kokei** Count, (cr. '96), b. July '51 at Kyoto; Lord Abbot of the greatest Buddhist centre in Japan, the East Hongwan Temple, for many years past a synonym of mismanagement, corruption and internal discord. The threat of bankruptcy was long hanging over the temple like the sword of Damocles, and quite recently even the princely residences and villas of the Lord Abbot were seized by a creditor. Fortunately things seem to have began to improve. Add. Higashi-Hongwanji, Kyo. — p. 128 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n173/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**OTORI, Keisuke** Baron (cr. 1900), Privy Councillor, b. Feb. '33 in Harima; was one of the ablest generals on the Shogunate side, in the civil war of the Restoration; but had to surrender with the future Adm. Viscount Enomoto to the Imperial troops after having defended Hakodate and was imprisoned for two years. (See "Enomoto"). Then appointed an official of the then created Hokkaido Colonization Board; next a Secretary of the Public Works Dep't now no more; President of the Peers' School, etc.; was Minister at Seoul when war broke out between Japan and China; removed to the present post shortly later. Is the President of the Businessmen's Corporation, Tokyo. Add. Torii-saka, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 129 ([scan leaf 174](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n174/mode/1up))

**OURA, Kanetake** Mem. House of Peers and ex-Minister of Communication, b. in Satsuma, May '50; Governor of Shimane-ken '63, afterward that of Yamaguchi, Kumamoto, Miyagi, &c.; Inspector-General of the Metropolitan Police '98-'00 and '01-'03; appointed to the Ministrial post Sept. 1903; is one of the ablest lieutenants of Marshal Yamagata, and a leader of the Daido Club. He holds the Grand Cordon of Rising Sun. Add. Kamakura, Kanagawa-ken. — p. 129 ([scan leaf 174](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n174/mode/1up))

**OYAMA, Iwao** Marshal, Marquis, (cr. Count '84, gr. Marquis '95) ex-Commander-in-Chief of the Manchurian Army, an ex-Chief of the General Staff; b. Oct. 42 in Satsuma, is a nephew of the great Saigo, and took some distinguishing part in the work of the Restoration; sent to Europe to study military tactics early in the era, and followed the Franco-Prussian war as attaché of the latter Army; had to experience bitterest pang in the Satsuma rebellion of '77 when his elder brother, as Governor of Kagoshima sided with the rebels and when he had to command a Brigade against his adoring uncle. He was promoted Lieut.-Gen. '78 and full General' 91, meanwhile surrounded by a staff brilliant young officers, undertaking a signal service in carrying our Army to the state of efficiency. On the occasion of the Japan-China War the General was appointed Commander-in-Chief of the Second Army which took Port Arthur and Wei-hai-wei; Marshal '98 and Chief of the General Staff, which post he soon resigned "to make room for the young talent" and succeeded by the late Viscount Gen. Kawakami, who may be said to have perfected the staff service to the present state of efficiency. His premature death obliged him, after the late Prince Komatsu filled the vacancy for a short while, to resume former post in the General Staff till the time of the outbreak of the late war in which he, with the help of his second in Command, Gen. Kodama, and staff of brilliant officers, did carry the Imperial armies to the pinnacle of fame. The Marquis bears the 2nd class of the Golden Kite and the Grand Order ('02) and for service in the late war he was granted 1st class Golden Kite and Collar of Chrysanthemum. Bestowed Order of Merit by King Edward. Marchioness Oyama was one of the young ladies who were first despatched by the Government for study to U.S.A. in '71, and is a grad. of Vassar Col. Add. Aoyama, Tokyo. — p. 129 ([scan leaf 174](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n174/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**OYAMA, T'sunasuke** ex-Japanese Minister at Rome, b. '53 at Kagoshima; was at Legation Chancellor '78-'79 at Rome and Paris; promoted Secretary of the Legation at London '84; personal secretary to the Foreign Minister '86; transferred to Secretary of Legation at Paris '88; appointed Minister at the Hague '92, then at Vienna, and finally at Rome, '99 till '06. — p. 130 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n175/mode/1up))

**OZAKI, Saburo** Baron, (cr. '96), Mem. House of Peers since '90, b. 42 in Kyoto-fu; held various posts at the Home Office and Cabinet, the last post he held being that of the Director of Legislative Bureau '90. Was a promoter of the Seoul-Fusan R'ly. His service rendered in connection with the Restoration was rewarded with Peerage. Add. Azabu Roppongi, Tokyo. — p. 130 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n175/mode/1up))

**OZAKI, Yuki-o** M.P., of unbroken record, Mayor of Tokyo, b. '59 in Miye-ken; studied at Keiogijuku and for a short while held post of jun. Secretary at Central Gov., leaving which he soon attracted public notice as one of those prominent politicians opposed to the clan Gov. and swore to introduce party politics. In '87 he was ordered to leave Tokyo by the coup d'etat; he utilized this opportunity for purpose of visiting England whence he returned home on the eve of the summoning of the 1st session of the Diet to sit in ever since. He was chief councillor of the Foreign Office '97, when the short-lived entente was concluded between the Matsukata Cabinet and the Progressives; was the Minister of Education in the Okuma-Itagaki Coalition Cabinet, but had to resign from a trivial cause but leading to his severing connection with Count Okuma's party with which he had been identified since its inception. He next joined Marquis Ito's new party and made its leader in the House but in '03 he resigned the Constitutionalists, so that he is now a free lance; elected Mayor of Tokyo the same year; in '05 married as second wife Miss Theordore Ozaki, daughter of Baron Ozaki by English lady. Add. Shinagawa, Tokyo. — p. 130 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n175/mode/1up))

**SAIONJI, Kin-mochi** Marquis, (cr. '84, formerly courtier), Premier and Leader of Seiyukai, b. Oct. '49 at Kyoto, young brother to Marquis Tokudaiji (Lord Chamberlain) and adopted as heir of the present house. Early attracted notice for precocious genius, having been allowed to take part when under 20 in the grave councils culminating in the rehabilitation of the Imperial authority. At 19 he was Commander-in-Chief of an Imperial Army; Gov. of Niigata for a few months in '68; and was dispatched abroad for study to France '69-'80. Brought home highly democratic notions that startled his seniors, issuing a democratic paper called "Oriental Liberty" with the late Mr. Nakaye and Mr. Matsuda, M.P. (Min. of Justice) which he had to discontinue in deference to the strong objection of senior Peers of conservative ideas. Then commenced his official career, first as Vice-Senator '81; accompanied Marquis Ito to Europe and U.S.A. to investigate Parliamentary system '82; Senator '83; Minister to Austria '85; then moved to a similar post at Berlin '88; President of the Board of Decora- tion '91; Vice-Pres. Code Investigation Commission and Vice-Pres. House of Peers '93; Privy Councillor '94; held chair of Education in the 2nd Ito Cabinet '94-'96 temporarily acting as Min. of Foreign Affairs in consequence of the late Count Mutsu's illness; Min. of Education in the 3rd Ito Cabinet and was nominated President of the Privy Council on the death of Count Kuroda, and as President he had to occupy post of Prime Minister ad interim no less than three times during the interval between the resignation of one Ministry and the formation of another. When Marquis Ito with whom he organized the Seiyukai had to enter the Privy Council July '03, the subject of the sketch exchanged post with him and became Leader of the Seiyukai. On the fall of the Katsura Ministry on account of public discontent about terms of the peace, the retiring Premier recommended the Marquis as his successor and he was installed in the chair Jan. '06. His younger brother is the head of great Sumitomo family by whom he was adopted. Add. Nagata-cho, Tokyo. — p. 130 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n175/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**SAITO, Minoru** Vice-Admiral, Minister of the Navy, b. '58 in Iwate-ken; entered the Naval Academy; Sub.-Lieut. '82; was Naval attaché to the Japanese Legation at Washington '84-'86 to be attached to the Naval Staff Board as Lieutenant; Staff officer of the Standing Squadron '90 to be attached again soon after to the said Board; acting Com. of Takao '92. At the outbreak of the Japan-China War he held post of naval aide-de-camp to His Majesty; transferred to be Vice-Commander of the cruiser Izumi and then to Chief Staff Officer in charge of the Standing Squadron, and was rewarded with 4th class Golden Kite for his servicee in the war: undertook as second in command the task of bringing home from England the battleship Fuji '96. Lieut.-Commander '97, and Captain '98 in command first of Akitsushima on which he cruised to Manila the same year and next of Itsukushima. Appointed the same year Vice-Minister of the Navy under Adm.
