Col., '81, and was appointed clerk in Dep't of Agr. and Com. and compiled the Japan Marine Fauna catalogue, fishery having long been his favorite study; crossed over to America and stayed till '88 during which he studied at Amerhest Univ. and also investigated education for lunatics at Pensylvannia Lun. Hos'l. Was appointed teacher at the Tokyo High School, and by his refusal to bow before the Emperor's portrait from religious scruple he had to resign. Subsequently he has fought hard as non-sectarian Christian preacher, journalist and educationist, fighting with almost Carlyean vehemence. Has written "How I became a Christian" (in English), and other works, and is now publishing the monthly magazine, "Study of the Bible." Add. Tsunohazu, near Tokyo. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n199/mode/1up))

**UCHIYAMA, Kojiro, Maj.-Gen** and Mil. Attaché to the Japanese Legation at St. Petersburg, b. '59 in Tottori; Sub. Lieut. (Art.) '79; was attached to the Legations in Russia and France; was rewarded with 4th class Golden Kite in the Japan-China War, and was Com. Art. Corps attached to the 2nd Army during the late War. Was granted 2nd Class Golden Kite and of Rising Sun (war honor.) — p. 154 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n199/mode/1up))

**UKITA, Kazutami** historian and educationist. Prof. Waseda Univ., b. '54 in Kumamoto; studied English under Capt. James of U. S. A. engaged by the Lord of the fief and was converted into a Christian; entered the Doshisha '76 and grad. '79 and was for same while engaged in religious work; Prof. at his alma mater; entered Yale 92 and studied theology and politics; was again Prof. at the Doshisha and next at present institution '96. Add. Yak-oji, Ichigaya, Tokyo. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n199/mode/1up))

**UME, Kenjiro** Hogaku-hakushi. Prof. of Law at the Imp. Tokyo Univ., b. '60 in Shimane-ken; studied law in France and Germany '86-'89 after grad. from the Imp. Tokyo Univ., at which he has been teaching civil and com. laws since '90; appointed Dir. of Legislative Bureau '97 and Vice-Min. of Edu. '98. Is Legal Adviser to the Korean Government. Add. Hayashi-cho, Koi., T. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n199/mode/1up))

**UN-NO, Bisei** Prof. Gov. Fine Art Institute at Ueno, was b. '63 at Mito, and is noted as one of the master glyptists and casters, being a nephew of below. He has twice visited Europe and America. Add. Uyeno, Park. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n199/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**UNNO, Shomin** Prof. of the Gov. Fine Art Institute at Uyeno, b. '44 at Mito; is a master glyptist; an Art Commissioner to the Imp. Household. Add. Bamba-cho, Hj., Tokyo. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n200/mode/1up))

**URYU, Sotokichi** Vice-Admiral, Commander of the Takeshiki Naval Station, b. '54 at Kanazawa; entered the Navy early and was dispatched to America to complete study at Annapolis; Capt. '91; naval attache at the French Legation till '96; appointed Commander of the Akitsushima, Fuso, Yashima and others; Rear-Adm. '00 with a post at the Naval Staff Board; ordered to take the command of a detachment under Adm. Togo on the outbreak of the late War, the sinking of the Varyag and Korcetz at Ninsen on Feb. 9th by this detachment being well known. He took part in the battle of Aug. 14th (off Shantung) and in that of the Japan Sea, meanwhile promoted to Vice-Adm. The Adm.'s wife is a sister of Mr. Masuda, of the Mitsui, and was one of the pioneer girl students sent by Japan to U.S.A. for study, and is a Prof. of the Higher Normal Girls' School. The Adm. was granted 2nd class Golden Kite and Grand Corden of Rising Sun (war honor). Add. Takeshiki, Tsushima. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n200/mode/1up))

**USAGAWA, Kazumasa, Lieut.-Gen** Sectional Chief at the War Office, b. '49 in Yamaguchi, 4th son of T. Fujimura and adopted by K. Usagawa of the same clan; Lieut. '99. staff officer to the 1st Army in the Japan-China War which gained him the 4th class Golden Kite; Maj.-Gen. '01 and promoted '06. He was granted 2nd class Golden Kite as war honor. Add. Minami Teramachi, Y.. T. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n200/mode/1up))

**USHIBA, Takuzo** b. '50 in Miyeken; held a clerical post at the Home Office '76; appointed Adv. to the Korean Gov. '82-'83; Director of the Taxation Bureau, Treasury '83-'87 when he left officialdom to be appointed a Dir. and in time Man. Director of the Sanyo Rly. lately nationalized. Was a M.P. '92-93. Add. Sanyo Rly. Co., Kobe. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n200/mode/1up))

**UYEHARA, Yusaku, Lieut.-Gen** b. '56 in Hyuga and adopted into the present family; Sub.-Lieut. (Eng.) '79, was ordered to study in France '89 staff office to the 1st Army in the Japan-China War which gained him 4th class Golden Kite; attended the Coronation Ceremony of the Tsar and also Hague Peace Conference; during the late War was chief staff to Marshal Nozu whose eldest daughter is his wife. Was granted 2nd class Golden Kite and Rising Sun as war honor. Add. Daimachi, Akasaka, T. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n200/mode/1up))

**WADA, Hikojiro, Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, M.P** b. '59 in Hiroshima-ken; took active part in the democratic movement under Count Itagaki about 1880; visited Europe and America '89-'90; was identified for several years with the former Nationalist Association; appointed Dir. of the Agri. Bureau, Ar. and Com. Dep't '97-'03; transferred to the Com. and Ind. Bureau. and then raised to the present post. He has sat in the House without break since '92. Add. Kobiki-cho, Tokyo. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n200/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**WADA, Tsunashiro** b. '56 in Fukui-ken. is one of the foremost mineralogists and geologists of Japan; Secr. of the Dep't. Agr. and Com., and promoted Dir. of Mining Bureau; undertook the founding of the Gov. Steel Works at Wakamatsu '97-'98; has since been engaged in gold mining work. Add. Sanai-saka, U., Tokyo. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n201/mode/1up))

**WAKAO, Ippei** b. in Yamanashi-ken. '20; and acquired the present affluence by dealing in raw silk early in the era; Member House of Peers for the highest-tax-payers of his native district; founded a family bank (Wakao Bank) and is connected with several business establishments. Add. Kofu, Yamanashi-ken. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n201/mode/1up))

**WAKATSUKI, Reijiro** Vice-Min. of Finance, b. '66 in Shimane, to be adopted into the present family '86; grad. Law of the Imp. Univ. '92; was appointed councillor of the Treasury soon after graduation where he rapidly rose to be made a Bureau Dir. '03, and then to the present post on the formation of the present Cabinet (Jan. '06). Has been appointed Financial Agent stationed abroad. Add. Yarai, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n201/mode/1up))

**WATANABE, Chiaki** Baron. (cr. 1900), Treasurer and Dir. of Estate Bureau of the Imp. Household, b. '43 in Nagano-ken; took some active part in the work of Restoration and held the post of Sec. at Kagoshima at the time of the outbreak of that disastrous civil war; subsequently appointed Gov. of one prefecture or another, till appointed Vice-Min. of Home Office '92; is reputed as being one of the wealthiest among public servants. He is an elder brother of Vis. K. Watanabe (which see). Add. Takanawa, Tokyo. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n201/mode/1up))

**WATANABE, Kunitake** Viscount (cr. '96), ex-Minister of Finance, b. '46 in Nagano-ken; was long connected with the Treasury till he rose to be Vice-Min. '89-'92 and next to the principal post '92-'94. In consideration of his service during the Japan-China War he was created Viscount; was temporarily transferred to the chair of Communications but soon restored to the original chair of Finance which he left '95. He was Marquis Ito's right-hand man when he undertook the organization of the "model party" '00, having held the post of chairman of the Organization Committee. Some estrangement took place between the two, for the Vis't resigned the chairmanship; but when the Marquis organized his Ministry he was given the chair of Finance. The Ministerial rupture over the question of financial adjustment, and the retrenchment insisted upon by the Viscount culminated in the miserable collapse of the Cabinet. The adoption by the succeeding Finance Minister of a retrenchment measure similar to that proposed by the subject of this sketch seems to indicate that he was in the right in that notorious Ministerial dissension. The Viscount is a great votarist of the Buddhist doctrine of asceticism, and wrote a little work on Indian philosophy. Add. Azabu Hommura-cho, T. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n201/mode/1up))

**WATANABE, Noboru** Viscount, (cr. '87), b. '38; long connected with the civil service as local Gov. and lastly the Chief of the Board of Audit '84-'00. He is famous as a master of sword- manship. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n201/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**WATANABE, Senjiro** Man. Dir. Mitsui Bussan, b. '58 in Tokyo, a son of a doctor; studied commerce at Mr. Yano's Commercial School (forerunner of present Higher Com. School) and the now defunct Mitsubishi Com. School; entered the Mitsui Bussan '79, to be sent soon afterward to its London Branch of which he had charge of for over 20 years till his return home '02 to take post at the head office. Mrs. Watanabe is an English lady. Add. Shimmachi, Akasaka, T. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n202/mode/1up))

**WATANABE, Shotei** b. in '51 at Tokyo, and is celebrated for his delicate style of painting, landscapes, flowers, birds, etc.; an Art Commissioner to the Imp. Household. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n202/mode/1up))

**WATASE, Shozaburo** Prof. of the Imp. Tokyo Univ., b. at Numazu in '63; grad. Sapporo Agr. Col. '84, and after studying for a little while his special subject of zoology at the Imp. Tokyo Univ., was despatched to John's Hopkin's Univ. by the Gov.; appointed Lecturer by the Chicago Univ. where he remained till 1900, when he returned home and was soon after given the present chair at the Univ. here. Add. Doshimmachi, Koi., T. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n202/mode/1up))

**YAMAGATA, Aritomo** Marshal, Marquis, (cr. Count '84 and gr. Marquisate '95), Pres. of Privy Council, holder of Grand Cordon, one of the elder statesmen, was b. April '38 in Choshu, and was one of the ablest captains when the clan of Choshu had to defend itself against the invasion of the Army of the Tokugawa Shogunate a year before the Imp. Gov. was restored to power. He was a chief staff officer who practically directed the operation when the Imp. Army was sent against the clan of Aizu, the last champions of the fallen Tokugawa; appointed a Major-Gen. and a junior Vice-Minister of War by the Imp. Government soon after restoration of tranquility. Promoted Lieut.-Gen. '72 and the Minister of War '73; was chief of staff of the Army of subjugation commanded by the late H.I.H. Prince Arisugawa in the civil war '77. After having sat frequently in the chair of the Chief of the General Staff and of War,circumstances obliged him to undertake various civil posts, chiefly connected with Home Affairs and Justice Department. Visited Europe and America '88-'89 to study the local self-government system and the constitution of civil corporations. Was sent to the front as commander of the 1st Army, in the Japan-China War, but soon compelled by ill-health to return home and was granted Marquisate and the 2nd Order of the Golden Kite. Attended the Coronation ceremony of Tsar Nicholas '96, and brought home the Yamagata-Lobanow Convention (see Chap. "Diplomacy"), in regard to Korea. He undertook the arduous task of forming a Ministry '89-'91, and his long military custom of strict discipline landed him more than once into scrapes with the freedom-loving members, for from his long military career, he hates political parties and is a pronounced opponent of the principle of party Cabinet. He was therefore opposed to Marquis Ito's proposal to recommend the Opposition leaders, Counts Okuma and Itagaki, to form a ministry as successors, and the Marshal and his followers secretly watched an opportunity to pull down the party ministry, and the latters' internal dissension soon enabled them to attain their purpose. The Marshal, under the circumstances, was obliged to undertake the task of forming a Ministry '98-'00, and his second Ministerial career was not a success. The Marshal's political career outwardly ended with this second Premiership, but he still continued to wield potential power in active politics. The late Katsura Cabinet was practically a Yamagata Ministry except in name, the ex-Premier himself being one of his most trusted lieutnants, for the Marshal's strong magnetic power combined with his paternal kindness has created a host of followers. His conservative ideas are naturally opposed to the principle held by Marquis Ito, and the recent ministerial changes may therefore be said as outcome of rivalry between the two statesmen. On the outbreak of the late War the Marshal succeeded Marshal Oyama as Chief of the General Staff, to be transferred to present position on Marquis Ito's departure for Korea as Resident-Gen. The Marchioness died some years ago and he has been a widower since. He has adopted as heir, as he had no issue, one of his sister's sons, the present Min. of Communications. (V. "Marquis Ito.") He was made member of the Order of Merit by King Edward, while the service in the late War was rewarded with 1st Class Goden Kite and Collar. Add. Sekiguchi, Koi., T. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n202/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**YAMAGATA, Isaburo** Minister of Com. b. '57 in Choshu; held various subordinate posts in the Central Government and served as Gov. in several provinces; appointed to the present post soon after the formation of the Saionji Ministry; and given present chair in deference to his powerful uncle and father-in-law, he being an adopted son and heir of Marshal Marquis Yamagata, and son of his sister. Add. Kobiki-cho, Tokyo. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n203/mode/1up))

**YAMAKAWA, Kenjiro** Rigaku-hakushi, Mem. House of Peers, b. July '54 at Aizu; studied physics in America and Europe, was connected with the Tokyo Imp. Univ. from his return in '72; resigned '05 President of Imp. Tokyo Univ. dissatisfied with interference of executive authorities (v. "Tomizu"). Add. Hatsune-cho, Koi., T. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n203/mode/1up))

**YAMAMOTO, Gombei** Mem. of Supreme Mil. Council, Admiral, Baron, (cr. '02), b. Oct. '52 in Satsuma and fought in the War of the Restoration on the Imperial side; one of the pioneer graduates of the Naval Academy which he left '77; was on board a naval training vessel that visited America and then made a voyage round the world on board a German warship; promoted to Lieut. '80 and appointed Vice-Commander of the cruiser Asama; was one of the commissioners sent to England '85 to bring home the cruiser Naniwa built there; Commander of g.b. Amaki '86; was dispatched to Europe and America in '87 on suite of the then Vice-Minister of the Navy (Adm. Kabayama); Captain '90 and commander of Takao and next of Takachiho. He was soon transferred to the Naval Office first as Personal Secretary of the Minister; promoted as Director of a Bureau and was gazetted Rear-Admiral '95. Vice-Minister '96 when he was promoted to Vice-Adm. and Minister from '98 to Jan. '06, having been appointed full Admiral '04. He was granted 4th Class Golden Kite in connection with the Japan-China War during which he was at Hiroshima as personal Secretary to the Minister of the Navy. For his service in the late War he was granted 1st Class Golden Kite and Grand Cordon. The Admiral is an able speaker and is statesman like capacity. Despatched to England on suite of Prince Fushimi '07 and was decorated G.C.M.G. by King Edward. Add. Takanawa, Tokyo. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n203/mode/1up))


## Who's Who

**YAMAMOTO, Kametaro** b. '47 at Osaka; is the greatest Japanese tea-merchant in Kobe, having made the present fortune through his direct export of tea early in the era. He is the chairman of the Kobe Chamber of Commerce and is a leading figure in the business circles of Kobe and Osaka. Add. Kobe. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n204/mode/1up))

**YAMAMOTO, Tatsuo** Mem. House of Peers, ex-Gov. of the Bank of Japan, b. '56 in Oita-ken; connected with educational work from '80 to '82 when he entered the Mitsubishi Firm and was appointed a Director of the Yusen Kaisha which was created on the amalgamation of the Mitsubishi with the rival company; entered '90 the Bank of Japan in which he was appointed Dir. of Banking Bureau, to be nominated Governor '98; relieved of the post '03 when the term expired; nominated Mem. Upper House in Nov. of the same year. Add. Kami-Niban-cho, Koj., T. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n204/mode/1up))

**YAMANO-UCHI, Ichiji** Dir. of the Rly. Affairs Bureau and Vice-Pres. of the Imp. Railways, was b. '67 in Kagoshima; grad. in '90 Law of the Tokyo Imp. Univ.; appointed Governor of Aomori-ken '01; Director of the Railway Bureau '03; Vice-President in '07 as additional post. Add. Gov. Railways Bureau, T. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n204/mode/1up))

**YAMASE, Sho-in** Prof. of the Music Acad. at Uyeno, b. '45; is a master-player of Japanese Koto. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n204/mode/1up))

**YAMAWAKI, Gen** Mem. House of Peers and President of Adm. Litigation Court., b. in Fukui '49, eldest son of a retainer of the clan; studied medicine at the Medical School of the fief and is one of the few remaining pupils of Rev. Dr. Griffis, author of "Mikado's Empire" etc. who was engaged as instructor by the Prince. Subsequently proceeded to Nagasaki to pursue his study and learn Dutch. Sent to Germany '70 and studied law at Berlin, returning '72. He afterward filled various high civil posts and was appointed to the present post in Jan. '06. Mr. Haruki Yamawaki, Sectional Chief and Dir. of Com. Museum, Agri. and Com. is his adopted son and heir. Add. Hinoki-cho, Aka., Tokyo. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n204/mode/1up))

**YAMAZA, Enjiro** Dir. Political Bureau, Foreign Office; b. '66 in Fukuoka, grad. Law, Imp. Tokyo Univ., '92, entered the Foreign Office soon after and served abroad for a number of years, at the Legations and Consulates in London, Seoul etc.; was appointed the present post '01. Was on the suite of the Peace Plenip. '05 and of the Plenip. to China '06. Add. Nagata-cho Nichome, T. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n204/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**YANO, Fumio** b. '50 in Oita-ken; has had a varied career; first began his active life as Sec. of Finance '78-'81 and won the confidence of his chief, Count Okuma, with whose resignation he left office and was for some while a leader of the political party organized by the Count; wrote a political novel delineating the life of Ancient Greece and it enjoyed a great popularity; he visited Europe and America, and on returning became chief editor of the Hochi; entered the Imperial Household '90 as Dir. of Imp. Archieves; removed to Min. to Peking '97-'99. He has lately returned to journalism; and has somewhat turned to a socialist philosopher. He is more a man of letters than a politician. Add. Harajuku, Aoyama, T. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n205/mode/1up))

**YASHIMA, Kajiko** a famous lady educationist, b. '34 at Kumamoto; principal of the Joshi Gakuin (Girls' School) which she founded in Tokyo about '95; has devoted herself to the work of elevating the ideal of women and of reclaiming the erring sisters; one of the principal promoters of the Society for the ed the World's Conference of the Society held in U.S.A. last year. Add. Kami-niban-cho, Koj., T. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n205/mode/1up))

**YASUDA, Zenjiro** is millionaire who sprang from obscurity to the present affluence, greatest perhaps next to the Mitsui and the Mitsubishi; b. Oct. '38 in Toyama-ken, and was an apprentice at a shop in Yedo and finally opened an exchange shop on his own account. This was enlarged in scope and elevated to a bank. He has founded several other banks and has moreover lent his powerful backing in the starting of the Tokyo Fire Insurance Co., the Imp. Marine Insurance Co. and the Kyosai Life Insurance Co. Being a public spirited man he does not spare to furnish fund to any useful undertaking. The fund required in the Osaka Harbor Works was almost wholly supplied by him. He is a business man of extraordinary integrity and diligence. Add. Yokoami-cho, Hj., T. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n205/mode/1up))

**YASUHIRO, Ban-ichiro** Mem. House of Peers, b. '59 in Fukuoka-ken; studied at Cambridge, England, for some years teacher at Kyoto High School; long held the secretaryship at the Cabinet, Home Office and others. Chief Secretary of the Yamagata Cabinet '98-'00, and held the post of Vice-Min. of Agr. and Com. '01-'03. Add. Nishikata-machi, Hg., T. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n205/mode/1up))

**YODA, Hyaksen** Chinese scholar, b. in Sakura; took part in the Restoration movement and on the reinstatement of the Imp. Government was given office of Secretary in the then Cabinet, Edu. Dep't etc. Resigned service '85, to devote himself to literary pursuit, and has written a number of dramas. Add. Ogawa-machi, Kanda, T. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n205/mode/1up))

**YOKOI, Tokio** M.P., journalist and author b. '57 in Kumamoto, eldest son of the celebrated Heishiro Yokoi who was assasinated early this era; studied English at Kumamoto and was one of the first graduates of the late Neejima's Doshisha of which he was made Pres. '97 but left '99 owing to the dispute with the foreign mission board. He twice visited abroad and studied philosophy at Yale '94-'96, but with his resignation of the post at his alma mater he turned a politician indentified with the Seiyukai, and shortly held a post of Councillor in the Communication Dep't. Was connected with the Nichi Nichi and visited India '03, and was returned from Okayama-ken in the last general election. Add. Higashi Okubo, near T. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n205/mode/1up))


## Who's Who in Japan

**YOKOTA, Kuniomi** President of the Supreme Court, b. '50 in Oita-ken; entered the juridicial service '76, Public Procurator '80, then acting Dir. Criminal Bureau and Sec. of the Dep't which post he resigned to study law in Europe '86-'91 to be appointed on return Pub. Pro. of the Tokyo Appeal Court, Councillor then Vice-Min. of Justice '96, Soli.-Gen. '98 Chief Pub. '99, Soli.-Gen. '04, and has been appointed the present post June '06. Add. Kagurazaka, U., T. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n206/mode/1up))

**YOSHIDA, Kosaku, ex-Man. Dir** Tokyo R'ly Co. & Dir. of several other concerns. b. '55 in Tokyo, son of a wealthy pawnbroker; succeeded to the business when only 20 and restored the prosperity of the house that had began to decline; promoted the Tokai Bank '90. Add. Domei-cho, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n206/mode/1up))

**YOSHIHARA, Saburo** Vice-Min. of Home Affairs, b. '54 in Chiba-ken; entered the former Law School, Justice Dep't '76 but had to leave it without finishing the course owing to illness; a Jun. Judge '77-86 at various district courts; entered the House of Rep. as Sec. '90; then Tokyo Imp. Univ. '88 to study law and finished the course the following year; appointed a Secretary of the House of Representatives '90; then Secretary in Saitama, Osaka and Aichi, to be promoted to the Governor of Kagawa and next of Okayama, till he was transferred to the Home Office as Director of Local Administration Bureau '03 and to the present post in Jan. '06. Add. Ichiban-cho, Koj., T. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n206/mode/1up))

**YOSHIKAWA, Akimasa, Viscount** (cr. '96), ex-Minister of Home Affairs, Mem. House of Peers, b. '41 at Tokushima; entered the civil service early in the reign, and after having held various posts in the Treasury, Home Office and others was appointed the Gov. of Tokyo and then Vice-Min. of Home Affairs. Then began his ministerial career, first as Minister of Edu. '90, Justice '93-'96, Home Office '91-'96, Communications twice '98-'00 and '01-'03, Home Office '04, which last he had to resign in connection with the Sept. anti-Peace Agitation in Tokyo. Add. Azabu Miyamura-cho, T. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n206/mode/1up))

**YOSHINO, Seikei** b. '49 at Tokyo, undertook for long a conspicuous part in the municipal work of Tokyo, and repeatedly occupied the chair of the Municipal Council. Sat for the city in the House '90-'92. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n206/mode/1up))

**YURI, Kinmasa, Viscount** (Cr. '87), Mem. House of Peers, b. '29 at Fukui; filled with credit the arduous post of Treasurer to his clan shortly before the Restoration and that of Min. of Finance of the Imp. Gov. after that grand work had been consummated. It was chiefly through his persevering efforts and judicious management that the Gov. could pay its way at the initial stage of its restoration. He was not a mere financier, but a statesman as well, for the celebrated "Five Articles of Oath" concerning the internal reform publicly sworn by the Emperor at his coronation was a result of the Viscount's memorialization. Has held aloof from active politics long since. Add. Shinjiku, Tokyo. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n206/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**ZUMOTO, Motosada** editor of the Seoul Press, b. '62 in Hoki Province, and grad. Sapporo Agr. Col. '84; was long on the staff of the Japan Mail; entered civil service for a short while and was twice appointed Per. Sec. to Marquis Ito when he was Premier; started the Japan Times '97 in conjuction with three other including the present writer, and it has served a useful purpose of conveying to foreigners Japanese view presented by native writers. Has traveled twice through Europe and America and was once a special correspondent of the London Standard. Accompanied Marquis Ito to Seoul as non-official mem. of the Residency-Gen., and in January last year he took over the Seoul Press formerly owned by some foreigners. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n207/mode/1up))

## The Japan Year Book

**1st Bank, Ltd** (The Dai Ichi Ginko).—Estab. in 1873 by Baron Ei-ichi Shibusawa and is the pioneer bank in Japan. It is the banking organ for the Korean Government and is authorized to issue paper notes for circulation in the peninsula, the volume at end of 1906 amounting to about yen 8,280,000.
President.—Baron E. Shibusawa.
General Manager. · Yunosuke Sasaki.
Head Office.—Kabuto-cho, Nihonbashi-ku, Tokyo. — p. 412 ([scan leaf 457](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n457/mode/1up))

**3rd Bank, Ltd** (The Dai San Ginko).—Estab. in 1876, it is practically owned by the Yasuda family.
President.—Zenshiro Yasuda.
Head Office.—Kobune-cho, Nihonbashi-ku, Tokyo. — p. 412 ([scan leaf 457](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n457/mode/1up))

**15th Bank, Ltd** (The Dai Jugo Ginko).—Estab. in 1877, enjoys the credit of commanding the largest amount of capital of all banks of ordinary kind. was formely called the Peers' Bank as it was created as the banking organ of the peerage circles.
President.—Kokichi Sonoda.
Manager.—Masayasu Naruse.
Head Office.—Kobiki-cho 1-chome, Kyobashi-ku. — p. 412 ([scan leaf 457](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n457/mode/1up))

**20th Bank, Ltd** (Dai Niju Ginko).—Estab. in 1877.
President.—Iken Kojima.
Head Office.— 2 Ise-cho, Nihonbashi-ku. — p. 412 ([scan leaf 457](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n457/mode/1up))

**27th Bank, Ltd** (Dai Niju-Shichi Ginko).—Estab. in 1877.
President.—Jiemon Watanabe.
Sub-Manager. Masami Muro-oka.
Head Office.—Honzaimoku-gashi, Nihonbashi-ku. — p. 412 ([scan leaf 457](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n457/mode/1up))

**100th Bank, Ltd** (Dai Hyaku Ginko).—Estab. in 1877. One of most prosperous banks with wide foreign connection. Its capital increased to Y.2,000,000 in Feb. '07.
President.—Kojiro Takada.
General Manager.—Kenzo Ikeda.
Head Office.—Yorozu-cho, Nihonbashi-ku. — p. 412 ([scan leaf 457](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n457/mode/1up))

**Higo Ginko, Ltd** —Estab. in 1877 and may be regarded as the joint property of Marquis Hosokawa and the Yasuda family.
Head Office.—Koami-cho, Nihonbashi-ku. — p. 412 ([scan leaf 457](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n457/mode/1up))

**Imamura Ginko** —Estab. in 1893 by the late Seinosuke Imamura.
Head Office.—Minami-Kayaba-cho, Nihonbashi-ku. — p. 412 ([scan leaf 457](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n457/mode/1up))

## Economic Corporations

### Leading Mercantile Corporations.

**Dai Nippon Beer Brewery Co** —(Formed in Jan. 1906 by amalgamating Ebisu, Sapporo and Asahi breweries, with Capital Y. 5,000,000) paid-up Capital 4,185,000; for 1st half, 1906. net profit Y. 396,597; for 2nd half '06 net profit 543,728 and dividend 15%per annum. President.—Kyohei Makoshi. Managing Director.—Chosaburo Uyemura. Head Office.—Meguro near Tokyo. — p. 427 ([scan leaf 472](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n472/mode/1up))

**Furukawa Copper Mining Co** —Late Ichibei Furukawa's Copper Works reorganized as such in 1905 with capital of Y. 5,000,000; works Ashio and six other copper mines besides Innai silver mine; also manufactures cokes. President.—Toranosuke Furukawa. Director.—Kunisuke Okazaki. Head Office.—Yaesu-cho, Kojimachi-ku, Tokyo. — p. 427 ([scan leaf 472](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n472/mode/1up))

**Fujita Firm** —Capital. Y. 5,000,000. (paid-up); run by Mr. Denzaburo Fujita and undertakes engineering work, works Kosaka silver and Zuiho gold mines, also reclamation of foreshore at Kojima, Okayama-Ken. Proprietor.—Denzaburo Fujita. Head Office.—Do-shima, Kita-ku, Osaka. — p. 427 ([scan leaf 472](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n472/mode/1up))

**Fuji Seishi Kwaisha** (Fuji Paper Mill Co.).—Estab. in 1887; Capital Y. 4,600,000. President.—Ichiro Murata. Head Office.—Sanjikken-bori, Kyobashi-ku, Tokyo. — p. 427 ([scan leaf 472](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n472/mode/1up))

**Hokkaido Tanko-Kisen Kwaisha** (Colliery and Steamship Co.).—The Company's Railways having been nationalized, it has changed its name to the present one, steamships taking the place of the railways. The Company has intered into contract with messrs. Vicker and Sons and Armstrong Co. to start conjointly steel foundry with the capital of Y. 10,000,000. This undertaking is under control of Vice-Adm. Yamanouchi, Commander of Kure Admiralty, indicating its special connection with the Navy. Managing Dir.—Kakugoro Inouye. Head Office.—Muroran, Hokkaido. Tokyo Branch.—Tsukiji. — p. 427 ([scan leaf 472](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n472/mode/1up))

**Meiji India-rubber Works** —Estab. in [illegible]; average daily employees about 130. Prop'tor.—Genjiro Komei. Office.—Kita-shinagawa near Tokyo. — p. 427 ([scan leaf 472](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n472/mode/1up))

**Mitsu-bishi Firm** —Capital Y. 5,000,000 (paid-up); run by the Iwasaki family and divided into three departments. banking (Y. 1,000,000); mining (working Ikuno silver, Sado gold, Takashima coal, Ozarusawa copper, Yoshioka silver etc.); Nagasaki dockyard. Managing Directors.—Mining Department, Dr. Kyugo Nambu; Nagasaki dockyard, Heigoro Shoda. Head Office.—Yaesu-cho, Kojimachi-ku, Tokyo. — p. 427 ([scan leaf 472](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n472/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Mitsui Bussan Co** —Founded in 1876; Capital Y. 1,000,000 (paid-up) represents sales and purchase department of the great Mitsui Firm. Managing Directors.—Senjiro Watanabe, Gi-ichi Iida, Kenzo Iwahara, Sankichi Komuro (London Branch) and Jotaro Yamamoto (Shanghai Branch.) Head Office.—Suruga-cho, Nihon-bashi-ku, Tokyo. — p. 428 ([scan leaf 473](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n473/mode/1up))

**Mitsui Mining Co** —Y. 2,000,000 (paid-up); undertakes working of Miike coal and sulphur deposits at Tsurugi-zan and Iwao. Managing Directors.—Takuma Dan, Yoshio Takahashi. Head Office—Suruga-cho. Nihombashi-ku, Tokyo. — p. 428 ([scan leaf 473](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n473/mode/1up))

**Mitsu-koshi Dry goods Store** - Capital Y. 500,000; paid-up Capital Y. 300,000; originally the dry-goods department of the Mitsui Firm, but subsequently detached as independent concern in which the Mitsui holds a large share. Managing Director. -O-suke Hibi. Head Office.—Suruga-cho, Nihombashi-ku, Tokyo. — p. 428 ([scan leaf 473](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n473/mode/1up))

**Nai-Koku Tsu-un Kwaisha** (Domestic Transport Co.)—Estab. in 1893; Capital Y. 1,250,000; paid-up Capital Y. 781,250; President. – Jimbei Yoshimura. Head Office.—Sanai-cho, Nihombashi-ku, Tokyo. — p. 428 ([scan leaf 473](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n473/mode/1up))

**Nippon Denki (Electric) Co** —Estab. in 1899; Capital by the Northern Electric Co's capitalists (N. S. A.) and a number of Japanese capitalists Y. 500,000 (fully paid-up). Directors.—K. Iwatare, R. C. Dodd. Head Office.—Mita-shikoku-cho, Tokyo. — p. 428 ([scan leaf 473](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n473/mode/1up))

**Nippon Seima Kwaisha** (Hemp Mill)—Practically controlled by the Yasuda and Okura; Capital Y. 2,000,000; dividend rate in '06 12% per annum; possesses factories at Osaka, Nikko, Otsu, and Kanuma and Sapporo. Amalgamated in May '07 with the Hokkaido Hemp Co., and capital increased to Y. 6,400,000. President.—Zensaburo Yasuda. Head Office.—Shinagawa-uragashi, Nihombashi-ku, Tokyo. — p. 428 ([scan leaf 473](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n473/mode/1up))

**Dai Nippon Seito Kwaisha** (Japan Sugar Refining Co. Ltd.) Reorganized in '06 by amalgamating the Nippon Seiseto and Nippon Seito (Osaka). Capital Y. 10,000,000; paid-up Capital Y. 7,500,000; dividend rate in '06, 20% per annum; owes the present prosperity to effort of Mr. Tozaburo Suzuki. President. -Dr. T. Sako. Managing Dir.—Otosuke Isomura. Head Office.—Onakigawa near Tokyo. — p. 428 ([scan leaf 473](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n473/mode/1up))


## Economic Corporations

**O-ji Seishi Kwaisha** (O-ji Paper Mill)—Estab. in 1897; Capital Y. 2,000,000 (paid-up); dividend in 1906, 8% per annum. Managing Director—Ume-shi-ro Suzuki. Head Office.—O-ji near Tokyo. — p. 429 ([scan leaf 474](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n474/mode/1up))

**Okura Co** —Engineering and Electric and other machineries. Prop'tor.—Kihachiro Okura. Directors.—Kokinji Takashima, Kumema Okura, C.Kadeno. Head Office.—Ginza Tokyo. — p. 429 ([scan leaf 474](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n474/mode/1up))

**Sakura Gumi** (Tanning and Leather works)—Capital Y. 1,000,000; paid-up Capital Y. 800,000; founded by the late Katsuzo Nishimura. Head Office.—Senju near Tokyo. — p. 429 ([scan leaf 474](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n474/mode/1up))

**Shiba-ura Engineering Work** —Run by the Mitsui Family with the Capital Y. 1,000,000 (Y. 820,000 paid-up); its daily employees number about 950. Managing Dir.—Jugoro O-taguro. Head Office.—Shibaura, Tokyo. — p. 429 ([scan leaf 474](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n474/mode/1up))

**Shimomura Dry Goods Store** (Dai Maru).—One of the largest dry goods stores in Japan, having been established several centuries ago. Prop'tor.—Masataro Shimomura. Head Office.—Tori-Hatago-cho, Tokyo. — p. 429 ([scan leaf 474](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n474/mode/1up))

**Suzuki Iron Works** —Estab. in 1891; employs about 500 operatives per day. Prop'tor.—Tozuburo Suzuki. Office.—Sunamura near Tokyo. — p. 429 ([scan leaf 474](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n474/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Dento Kwaisha** (Tokyo Electric Light Co., Ltd.)—Estab. in 1882; Capital Y. 7,150,000, paid-up; Dividend rate in 1906, 12%; has announced to increase its capital to Y. 18,000,000 in order to instal water power electricity plant, to be opened for service in autumn this year. President.—Sakutaro Satake. Head Office.—Yuraku-cho, Tokyo. — p. 429 ([scan leaf 474](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n474/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Gas Co., Ltd** —the only gas works in Tokyo first started as municipal concern in 1874 and sold in 1885 at Y. 29,000. Capital was only Y. 35,000 till 1894 to be subsequently increased to 8,400,000 of which Y. 6,300,000 is paid-up. Net profit in 1905, Y. 957,650, and Y. 1,058,494 in '06, Dividend rate 15% per annum. At end 1905 the pipes aggregated 456 miles. President.—Baron Shibusawa. Managing Directors.—Dr. Toyokichi Takamatsu; Ryosaku Kume. Head Office.—Nishiki-cho, Kanda-ku, Tokyo. — p. 429 ([scan leaf 474](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1907/page/n474/mode/1up))
